#6639. How the Holy Is Revered and Corrupted in The Body

Netflix’s buzzy new show, created by Quinn Shephard, examines guilt, God, and control through the eyes of teen girls attending a Catholic high school

I wanted nothing more than to see the Holy Spirit. One day after church, my grandfather described the moment he first believed was witnessing the Holy Spirit: a soothing, cleansing sensation in which God, in some way, possessed and reassured your mind. Divine intervention felt attainable for a moment. At first, I, too, hoped to discover serenity, an especially enticing thought since my parents were far less religious. But over time, that belief twisted in my preteen mind, instilling instead a fear that every misstep might damn me. To be viewed unfavorably by God—as a kid—is a terrifying thought. And it lingers.

I soon let go of the institutionalized beliefs. Yet, a longing to believe in something greater—especially when facing the unknown—never fully left me. I still desired to find spiritual ground, one free from this all-consuming, stress-fueling shame. This friction between spiritual surrender and self-actualization is the beating heart of Quinn Shephard’s The Body. Beneath a relentless downpour of religious guilt and an insatiable appetite for forgiveness, the students at St. Mark’s Catholic High School battle their inner demons as they discern what they want for themselves versus what the world expects of them.

Shephard and co-showrunner Gina Gammell thrust the audience into an age of Razor flip phones, AOL instant messaging, and Juicy Couture tracksuits, all set to a 2000s soundtrack featuring Kylie Minogue and No Doubt. Its barbed-edged storytelling draws comparisons to Cruel Intentions and Pretty Little Liars, with a bewitching touch of Jennifer’s Body or The Crucible. Here, girl teenagedom traverses suppressed sapphic love and the systemic weaponization of religious guilt—an incisive critique biting enough to solicit a petition with over 120,000 people pushing to cancel it. This coming-of-age series emerges from the dual nature of what we hold sacred: its capacity for reverence and its vulnerability to corruption.

Catholic school scares me. Teenagers scare me more, especially Lisbeth (Geena Meszaros), the hyper-religious, manipulative student at the heart of the story. At first, The Body introduces us to Lisbeth as the quintessential “weird kid,” socially ostracized because of her eyebrow-less face and her freakishly religious family. (She evokes Carrie in Carrie.) But as The Body unfolds, Lisbeth pulls—or rather, tugs—at the web of guilt ensnaring the school’s dance team, the Lions, concerning the near-death of their new teammate, Grace (Sofia Wylie). Grace fell into a coma shortly after the girls pretended to sacrifice her at a Halloween party, popping ecstasy pills they referred to as the “body of Satan.” This hazing ritual is all in good fun until Grace’s heart actually stops … if only briefly.

The aftermath casts a shadow of guilt over everything—especially for Anya (Kristina Bogic), queen bee and dance team captain, who moved Grace’s unconscious body back to her bedroom with Lisbeth’s help. Soon after, Anya begins to see visions of Grace in her dreams. The moment she confesses this to the group, the other girls start confirming visions of their comatose friend. This uncanny groupthink preys on the girls with something to hide, who carry shame with them wherever they go. Anya, for instance, feels deep contrition for her romantic feelings for her best friend Elise (Sarah Boustany), whereas Elise more boldly embraces her queer sexuality. Elise faces the world with a steady confidence, shielding her from spiritual panic—and its subsequent manipulation.

The same isn’t true for most of her teammates, all of whom seek solace in the intangible, in God and the Holy Spirit, in the saintly visions of Grace. Grace then becomes a symbol of the sacred, a story that inspires people and therefore can be corrupted to control others. The girls’ desperate need for forgiveness makes them vulnerable to Lisbeth’s manipulation. When the team goes on a getaway together, they take hallucinogenic mushrooms before deciding that they must purge evil spirits from Grace by way of an exorcism (led by Lisbeth, of course). Lisbeth controls this ritual, forcing each girl to confess their sins. (She already knows them all after reading all their private messages.) Her feigned omniscience incites hysteria among the girls.

Shame clouds nearly all the girls’ minds. Maddie (Shirley Chen) wrestles with an eating disorder, some girls admit to premarital sex or impure thoughts, and Lisbeth attempts to squeeze out a confession from Elise about her feelings for Anya. The Body runs the gamut of teenage girlhood, but Shephard distills these insecurities into a single critique of control: how the holy—the fear of God—can exploit people, corrupt what we see, and shape what we believe. In episode 6, “Star Quality,” mass hysteria breaks out in the town, with people associated with the church now believing to see visions and revere Grace as a saintly figure. It’s driven by an innate desire to be seen—to see something—and a desperate desire to be part of something larger.

This same desire, to witness something collectively transcendent, forged the foundation of my spiritual beliefs. It also scared me enough to run from them. A connection to God came with fear of hellfire and the shame of sin. In this way, forgiveness felt like something I received out of desperation, rather than salvation. The Body studies the dangers of this unmitigated belief, of a doctrine designed to parse out the unknown. But by whose authority? Shephard’s story reminds us that the storyteller—the pastor or the manipulative teenage girl—controls the things we see and the things we may believe.

#6638. The 25 Best Cocktails for Thanksgiving

With a whiskey warmer, spiced sour, or comforting classic in your hand, you can get through any awkward family gathering.

Are you ready for your annual awkward family gathering? Thanksgiving is finally here, and if thinking about safe topics to discuss with all three generations of your family is making you break out in hives, then rest assured we’ve got some recipes to give you the liquid courage to face it.

You’ve got the turkey basted and roasting in the oven. You’ve got your grandmother’s perfect mac and cheese. You’ve got mashed potatoes—the best. And you’ve got store-bought pie from your slacker cousin. But, wait, do you know what you’ll be drinking for the most important feast of the year? We promise: There’s something for everyone at your dinner table on this curated list of cocktails.

Does Grandpa only abide by the saying, “If it’s brown, drink it down?” We’ve got a few whiskey drinks that will keep him on ice. Did your aunt who drinks only French reds run through the bottle she brought already? There’s a sangria recipe that’ll have her too busy sipping to snip. Need a warm cup of something strong while you “take the dog for a walk”? There’s an Irish coffee that’ll keep your wits about you. So hurry up and add these cocktail recipes to your Turkey Day menu.

Here’s a whiskey sour that makes your fall-flavor dreams come true.

Add ingredients to a shaker and shake without ice. Pour into a glass and garnish with grated nutmeg and a dried apple slice.

This twist on the old-fashioned adds an autumnal flavor that will keep this classic feeling fresh.

Combine all ingredients into a mixing glass with ice. Stir until chilled and diluted. Strain into a rocks glass with a large cube. Garnish with an orange peel studded with cloves.

The delicious spice that goes into a pie doesn’t have to be just for lattes or baked goods.

Cover the rim of a stemless wineglass in simple syrup. Dip in cinnamon sugar and add ice to the glass. Pour in pumpkin-spice liqueur and Josh Cellars sauvignon blanc. Stir, then top off the glass with seltzer.

This three-ingredient cocktail is easy to whip up but will taste like it was complicated and time-consuming.

Mix cider and cinnamon whiskey in a Champagne flute. Top with Champagne, then stir lightly until incorporated. Garnish with an apple slice.

A sweet, fizzy sipper to enjoy before dinner.

Add all liquid ingredients to a stemless wineglass. Add ice. Stir to incorporate, then garnish.

A roasty, toasty espresso martini to enjoy after the big dinner.

Shake ingredients hard with ice until frothy, about 15 seconds. Double-strain into a chilled coupe or martini glass. Garnish with coffee beans, nutmeg, and cinnamon.

This sumptuous hot cocktail makes a great Thanksgiving treat.

In a mug, dissolve the sugar cubes in a little hot water. Add rum and butter. Fill the mug with hot water. Sprinkle nutmeg on top.

The margarita isn’t just a summer drink anymore.

In a cocktail shaker, combine all the ingredients with ice and shake. Rim a glass with cinnamon sugar and serve over rocks. Garnish with slices of lime and orange.

You need to keep the booze flowing to keep the mood light and the conversation going. So mix together this sangria recipe that’ll satiate any apple-cider lover.

Place apples, orange, and cloves in a large pitcher. Drizzle honey over the fruit, then add in apple cider, rum, ginger beer, and white wine. Stir to mix. Top with ice and let chill for 20 minutes before serving. Serve in glasses, including the chunks of fruit.

Whoever invented pouring bourbon into a cup of tea, we want to give you a big hug.

Boil water in a teapot. Steep star anise pod or tea bag inside. Add bourbon, honey, lemon, and Tiki bitters to a mug. Stir. Serve in glass mugs with a spoon and the teapot. To enjoy, pour tea over cocktail base to taste and stir.

This aperitivo is the perfect refreshment before you start serving those heavy Thanksgiving dishes.

Pour Campari and sweet vermouth into a highball glass filled with ice. Top with club soda. Garnish with an orange twist.

A balanced spin on the Manhattan that uses Scotch whisky, this cocktail will impress any fan of brown liquor.

Stir the Scotch, vermouth, and bitters well with cracked ice. Strain into a chilled cocktail glass. Garnish with a lemon twist.

Simple enough to whip up in under five minutes, this cocktail has enough Scotch to knock Grandpa under the table.

Stir the Scotch and amaretto well with cracked ice in a mixing glass. Strain over fresh ice into an old-fashioned glass.

If you’re a big fan of cranberry sauce (the one that comes in a can), you can cure your cranberry craving with this vodka cocktail.

Shake ingredients in a shaker with cracked ice. Strain into a chilled cocktail glass, then garnish with lime.

This green-tinged cocktail tastes fresh and slightly tart, but it’s boozy enough to chill you out after that heavy holiday meal.

Add all ingredients into a shaker. Shake well with cracked ice. Strain into a coupe glass. Garnish with a lime wedge and Luxardo cherries.

The Black Russian is the espresso martini’s cooler, more laid-back cousin. It’s so simple to make and yet deliciously complex in taste.

Stir ingredients with ice in a mixing glass. Strain into an old-fashioned glass over fresh ice.

Sometimes you don’t want to have to choose between two options, because you want to have it all! And you can have it both ways, with this gin-and-vodka martini, the vesper.

Stir ingredients briskly with ice in a mixing tin until very cold. Strain into a chilled cocktail glass. Garnish with a large, thin-cut lemon peel.

Why not turn your holiday gathering into an international experience? Incorporate this Russian classic into your cocktail rotation.

Squeeze lime juice into a copper mug (or a Collins glass) and drop in the spent hull. Add ice cubes, then pour in the vodka and fill with cold ginger beer. Serve with a stirring rod.

This rum cocktail will bring back fond memories of warmer weather.

Fill a tall glass with ice cubes. Add rum. Pour in ginger beer and lime juice. Stir with a barspoon, then garnish with a lime wedge.

Holidays are synonymous with Champagne, so pop open a bottle of bubbly and pour out one of these delicious cocktails.

Stir rum, lime juice, and honey thoroughly with cracked ice in a chilled cocktail shaker. Pour unstrained into a Collins glass. Top with Champagne.

#6637. Glove Shoes Are Having a Major Fashion Moment

The ultra-soft style is showing up everywhere, from ballet flats to polished heels and boots.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Glove shoes are certainly having a moment. Made from soft, pliable leather that molds to the foot, they often feature a higher vamp than traditional shoes and come in everything from flats to heels and boots. The style showed up across both the fall and spring runways, with high fashion designers giving it plenty of attention. Naturally, that makes us pay attention to it, too. Ahead, dive into the trend and shop it for yourself.

Surely you’ve heard of the jazz shoe trend by now, right? Some such styles are glove-like, but you can also find an abundance of “regular” ballet flats in a glove style. Personally, I can’t stop wearing the pair I have from Steve Madden.

If you really want to be the most fashion-forward person in your office (without looking like a clown), wear a pair of glove heels to your next board meeting. It’s a subtle swap that fashion insiders will immediately recognize. My personal favorites are from Bottega Veneta and 3.1 Phillip Lim.

Proenza Schouler Glove 70MM Leather Pumps

Now, when it comes to glove boots, your options are a bit more limited. That said, if any of these pairs speak to you, I recommend jumping on the chance to make them yours. Whether you go for a flat or heeled rendition, these are a sleek alternative to your average winter boot.

#6634. The 15 Most Thoughtful Personalized Gifts

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

These will make shopping easier than ever this year.

It might only be November, but the holidays are just around the corner, and with Hanukkah starting early this year on December 4, I’m already collecting gifts for everyone I love. Getting ahead of the curve has its perks: fewer frantic shipping fees, for one, and enough time to find something that feels genuinely special.

The easiest way to make a gift special? Get it personalized. Because who wants to tear through wrapping paper only to find the same thing everyone else is getting this season? Go the custom route, and even a classic gift feels like it was made just for them (because, well, it was).

Think engraved ID bracelets and signet rings for the guys, birthstone bracelets and sparkly initial pendants for the women in your life, and personalized sports merch for the fan whose team is a whole personality trait. Add sentimental photo albums full of family memories and a few delightfully quirky surprises they’ll never see coming, and you’ve got a list worth getting a head start on. Here are some of our favorite personalized gift ideas for the holidays this year.

Know someone who takes great pleasure in writing hand-written letters? Make their next correspondence even more exciting with personalized writing paper from Smythson. Customize everything from the size and color of the paper, the text and motif at the top of the paper, and the tissue lining and border on the envelopes.

Engraved jewelry is one of the classiest personalized gifts you can get for someone, especially when it’s simple and refined like this ID chain bracelet from Miansai. I got this bracelet for my boyfriend with his name engraved on it for the holidays last year, and he wears it pretty much every day.

A personalized jersey is a no-brainer for anyone with a favorite sports team. The Fanatics custom shop has merch for NFL, MLB, NBA, WNBA, NHL, MLS, and even college teams, so it really has something for every kind of sports fan. And it’s not just jerseys—there’s also custom drinkware, bags, accessories, and more.

Even if your giftee isn’t super into astrology, they’ll appreciate a candle that’s just for them (or, well, anyone that shares their birthday). If they are into astrology, they’ll love all the fun facts about their birthday on the candle’s label, including their Zodiac sign, element, constellation, tarot card, and more. And all you have to know is their birthday, so it’s a pretty easy gift.

Speaking of birthdays, birthstone jewelry is another classic gift that’s personalized by design. And all you have to know is their birth month to get it right. Brilliant Earth has a great selection of birthstone jewelry, from pendant necklaces and colorful earrings to gemstone tennis bracelets and rings. You can even add multiple birthstones to this eternity lab diamond ring, so you can personalize it with the birthstones of everyone in your family.

For the one who spends their weekends on the golf course: They’ll truly impress their golf buddies with a box of personalized golf balls. And it comes with tees, a divot tool, and poker-chip ball markers so they have everything they need.

If you have the time and want to do something really special, order one of these luxury lighters from S.T. Dupont and get it engraved in person at a local engraving shop or jewelry repair store. The extra effort will show, and your giftee will have something to carry with them wherever they go for years to come.

You’ll have fun playing around with all the features in Ralph Lauren’s online custom hat maker—we certainly have. Choose the color and add a name, logo, or monogram, and the result will be a one-of-a-kind baseball cap your giftee will wear all the time.

Don’t just print out a photo and call it a day; get it printed on glass for something truly unique. Upload your photo to Fracture and personalize it even more with colorful borders, templates, frames, and stands.

Ever gone into a comic book store and seen the walls of Funko POP action figures from every franchise you can imagine? Having cute action figures of all your favorite Star Wars characters is great, but imagine how cool it would be to give someone an action figure of themself. With Funko POP, you can do just that—and you’ll have fun building it. You can even get a pair of Funko POP figures, so you can include yourself in it, too.

If you’re looking for a romantic personalized gift for your partner, surprise them with a watercolor painting of the place where you had your first date or the spot where you got engaged. Whether it was a local coffee shop, beloved movie theater, or beautiful outdoor space, it’ll look good painted above your names.

In a world where everything is online and in the cloud, a physical photo album is all the more sentimental and special. Artifact Uprising makes putting together a photo album easy, and the result is high quality and beautiful. It’s something they’ll want to display on their coffee table for everyone to see.

Gifts based on birthdays is a common theme in this gift guide, but what can we say? It’s an easy way to personalize stuff. If they’re a history buff (or just love reading the news), they’ll enjoy flipping through this customized book of newspaper headlines from their birthday through the years.

You know that person who is absolutely obsessed with their dog or cat? (Okay, it’s me.) They’ll obsess over any gift that has to do with their beloved pet, like this custom sweatshirt embroidered with the face of their dog or cat. Just upload a photo of their pet (you can probably find one on their Facebook or Instagram) and choose a sweater color and size.

A customized L.L. Bean tote bag is something they’ll use all the time. It’s one of our favorite gifts of all time, and it’s an absolute classic.

#6633. Presenting: Ben Shelton and Trinity Rodman’s Relationship Timeline Amid Reports They’ve Possibly Split

For the past two years, one of the sport’s brightest young stars, Ben Shelton, has been in a relationship with Washington Spirit soccer champ Trinity Rodman—and despite their busy schedules, they seemed to be head over heels in love with each other. That is, until November 24, when the Daily Mail broke the news that the power couple reportedly split.

Since you’re probs wondering how this couple came to be (and what led to their rumored breakup), I’ve done the work of rounding up all of their biggest relationship moments in a little timeline for your pleasure. Read it and weep!

Ben hard launches their relationship on Instagram by sneaking a photo of Trinity kissing his cheek into a carousel post. Swipe to the end to see:

Soon after, Trinity clears up some of the discourse around her and Ben’s relationship—namely the story behind how they actually met—and makes a point to note that Frances Tiafoe, a friend of hers and Ben’s, did not introduce them to each other like he previously claimed.

“I can assure you Frances was not the reason we met or got together,” she said in an interview with ESPN. “I don’t know why he’s taking credit for that. Me and Ben can both cut that out. It was a solely me and Ben thing.”

For the record, their story went a little like this: “I was like, ‘Hey, when you gonna come visit me?’ and he was like, ‘I’ll come right now,’” Trinity explained in an Instagram video (below). “So then he flew out and the rest is history.”

By the end of the month, Ben appears on Trinity’s Instagram. In the photo, she leans back into Ben’s shoulder, while his face stays mysteriously hidden beneath a hat (despite her tagging him in the photo). Her caption? “Dibs 🤭.”

Ben also chimes in on clearing up the story on how he and Trinity got together, telling the New York Post, “Tiafoe had nothing to do with it. He’s taking credit, [saying] ‘Oh, yeah, I put them together. I was like Danny Ainge.’ He’s a liar. He’s trying to make it about himself. It’s not about him, but that’s funny.”

Ben gives Trinity prime placement on his feed with a super cute photo:

A few weeks later, Ben wishes Trinity a happy birthday on his Instagram page with a simple but sweet message that reads, “happy birthday beautiful girl, I love you.” Awwwww.

Trinity later gives her followers an even better look into her and Ben’s relationship, sharing a compilation video of them sweetly holding hands during their adventures.

Trinity is spotted in the stands with Ben’s family at Wimbledon.

And she later calls out Wimbledon commentators for mentioning her famous dad, Dennis Rodman, during Ben’s match:

Trinity roots for Ben on day one of the 2025 U.S. Open Tennis Championships, and literally pops out in the cutest ‘fit that consists of Ben’s face plastered all over her custom sweats.

Trinity writes the sweetest tribute message for Ben’s 24th birthday: “To the most beautiful soul I’ve ever met. Happy birthday Benny! You’ve changed me, loving me the way I never knew someone could. Even with your lifestyle you make me a priority every chance you get and I’ll always be grateful for every minute you love me. You inspire me everyday, and you always keep a smile on my face. Life with you is a dream. I love you NL❤️”

And Ben makes an appearance in the comments section, too, writing, “I love you ❤️.”

Trinity’s soccer team, Washington Spirit, is beaten by Gotham FC in a match. Ben consoles her on the side of the soccer pitch like the great boyfriend that he is.

Ben and Trinity’s relationship becomes wedding-guest official! The couple gets all gussied up for a friend’s wedding, and they look so good, it’s literally unfair.

In a lil change of format, Trinity takes her followers along for her swanky date with Ben—and there’s a really intimate moment where Ben pops into frame to kiss her neck (squeal).

Trinity also sits down with Cosmo for an interview and reveals that Ben urged her to temporarily step away from soccer amid her injury.

“When I was going through the injury [this past season]. I was very scared to step away from my team because I was scared they might think, ‘Oh, Trinity just thinks she can do what she wants. She can leave and focus on herself. That's selfish.’ And Ben was like, ‘Your health is everything. You could get hurt and be done playing soccer, then what?’” she recalled. “And so he just reminded me that I need to prioritize myself before it's too late. And he said: ‘You are the driver. If you want to turn left, you're gonna turn left. Do you want to crash or do you want to keep going on the road?’”

She notes that birthdays and large celebrations can be difficult for her and Ben to do on the actual day, likely due to their sporty commitments. She added, “For us, it's so hard to celebrate literally anything. Even for our birthdays, we celebrated them, like the week before and the week after. We celebrate on the phone. Thankfully for preseason, with Spirit, I'll be going to Florida, and he'll be there.”

Ben celebrates Trinity’s birthday with a carousel of images shared to his Instagram page, in true boyfriend style.

Ben plays in—and wins—the ATP 250 tournament. While giving a speech after his impressive title win, Ben publicly thanks Trinity for coming out, and sweetly calls her his good luck charm: “I wanna thank my girlfriend for flying here from Brazil. I won a title at I think every tournament that she's been at this year. You’re definitely the lucky charm.”

That same day, Ben posts a photo roundup to his Instagram, which features Trinity front and center.

Trinity, meanwhile, gives a closer look at her adorable Ben 10 tank top and reminds her followers that she is Ben’s No. 1 fan.

Wimbledon doesn’t go as planned for Ben (he was eliminated in the first round by Otto Virtanen in a shocking upset), but during the U.S. Open, he ends up besting Carlos Alcaraz in a five-set nail-biter.

And ofc, Trinity is there to show him all the love:

The Daily Mail breaks the news that—after almost two years together—Ben and Trinity have reportedly called it quits, noting that “their conflicting work schedules and constant travel made it too challenging for the couple to continue maintaining their relationship.” Luckily, sources add that Ben and Trinity are “believed to remain close.”

#6632. Louis Vuitton Takes Its Place at the Formula 1 Las Vegas Grand Prix Finish Line

A handcrafted trunk awaited the winner, Max Verstappen.

When Max Verstappen crossed the finish line at the Las Vegas Formula 1 Grand Prix on Saturday night, after a gripping race that saw the championship driver battle to maintain the tightest of leads against rivals George Russell and Kimi Antonelli, his trophy came in very special packaging: a bespoke Louis Vuitton trunk. For the past two seasons, Trophy Trunks have been presented to the winner of the annual Las Vegas event, which sees drivers race on a thrilling course that winds along the famous Strip. They have also been presented to the winners of the World Cup, America’s Cup, and the NBA Finals. Louis Vuitton had a strong presence across the whole race weekend, hosting a suite where guests including Teyana Taylor watched the events from above the track and were able to get an early look at the trunk.

The Las Vegas trunk was, like all Louis Vuitton custom pieces, made at Asnières-sur-Seine, a commune in northern France. It was handcrafted by 25 artisans, made to the specific dimensions of the trophy. The Trophy Trunks are a remarkable mingling of the old and the new. They use the traditional craftsmanship techniques that have defined the house for over 170 years. All construction of the trunks’ wood, leather, and locks was done with the same techniques LV has used since the beginning, meaning that in many ways the Trophy Trunks differ little from its trunks from a century ago. The modern energy of motor racing comes through via subtle touches like the two stripes across the trunk that make a large V, a nod to both Vuitton and victory—they’re black-and-white checkered as a nod to the Formula 1 finish line. A small badge that calls back to the early days of trunk travel and bears the winning driver’s initials was added this year.

Louis Vuitton’s relationship with Formula 1 began with the Grand Prix de Monaco, which the brand began creating Trophy Trunks for in 2021. In 2025, it became an official partner of the league, and this year, LV created trunks for all 24 of the season’s races. In early December, the Abu Dhabi Grand Prix will mark the final 2026 race.

#6631. Liberace’s Palm Springs Estate Hits the Market for $6.9 Million—Plus 4 More Luxury Homes for Sale

Also for sale this week: Annette Bening and Warren Beatty’s onetime home and the most expensive house in Charleston.

One of the most legendary entertainers in American history, Liberace dazzled audiences for decades with his over-the-top piano medleys, flamboyant costumes, and cheeky banter. The self-described “Mr. Showmanship” might be synonymous with Las Vegas, where he had a popular residency, but he also had a taste for Palm Springs, owning no fewer than four homes in the California desert oasis throughout his life.

Now, perhaps the most exceptional of those properties, a 1930s Spanish Revival estate—dubbed the Cloisters due to its network of arcades and courtyards—has hit the market for $6.9 million. Elsewhere in Southern California, the longtime Beverly Hills home of Hollywood power couple Annette Bening and Warren Beatty is also newly for sale, asking almost $15 million. And on the East Coast, Lady Gaga’s former penthouse atop Manhattan’s Hit Factory building is available for $7 million, while the most expensive home in Charleston just listed for $35 million. Check them all out below.

Acquired at the height of his career in the late 1960s, Liberace’s walled Spanish Colonial Revival estate in Palm Springs just hit the market for nearly $6.9 million. The famed entertainer turned the 1930s bed-and-breakfast, known as the Cloisters, into an extension of his opulent stage set, brimming with signature candelabras, opulent furnishings, and splashes of gold. He lived on the half-acre property until his death in 1987, after which new owners have completely remodeled the 8,000-square-foot home to bring it back to its original splendor. HGTV host Brett Waterman spearheaded the most recent update, filling the five-bedroom home with handcrafted details and understated furnishings. Perks include a heated in-ground pool, courtyards, and a spa facing the San Jacinto Mountains. It’s listed with Keith Markovitz and Rick Distel of TTK Represents at Compass.

On Wednesday, ELLE Decor exclusively reported that the longtime Beverly Hills home of Warren Beatty and Annette Bening was hitting the market for the first time since they sold it. The Hollywood power couple custom-built the gated residence in 1992 and raised their four kids there, selling it in 2012 to hotelier Jonathan Baker and his wife, Jenny. They spearheaded their own revamp to the tune of $5 million, bringing a contemporary spirit to the interiors of the nine-bedroom compound, which includes a guest house, pool, and tennis court. Its new asking price is $14.995 million.

Last year, a 225-year-old mansion smashed the record for the most expensive home sold in Charleston, fetching a little over $21 million. Now, another estate is vying for the title. The Sword Gate House, located in the South Carolina city’s historic South of Broad neighborhood, just listed for $35 million with Leslie Turner of Maison Real Estate. Revamped by interior designer Cortney Bishop, the 17,140-square-foot mansion dates to the early 1800s and features preserved millwork, exposed brick, and an original mural in the dining room depicting historic Charleston harbor. There are also uncommon perks like a triple-decker piazza, a ballroom, a pool, and an acre of manicured gardens. The whole compound is secured behind preserved wrought-iron gates made by local ironworker Christopher Werner, giving the landmark its moniker.

Forget a “Million Reasons”—we’re looking at $7 million. The New York City penthouse where Lady Gaga once lived just hit the market for nearly $7 million—and she’s not the home’s only celebrity connection. The 3,700-square-foot duplex was also once home to former NBA star Carmelo Anthony and 30 Rock actor Tracy Morgan, per People.

With its organic silhouette, cantilevered roofline, and clever use of space, Leuthold House by Allan Gelbin bears the hallmarks of his mentor, legendary architect Frank Lloyd Wright. Completed in 1966 on five rolling acres in New Canaan, the five-bedroom house spans roughly 5,700 square feet and underwent a restoration by Hobbs Inc. and JMK Architects in 2014. The interiors feature long expanses of glass and mahogany, while the grounds come with a spring-fed pond, a pool, a one-bedroom guest house, and 150 specimen trees. Listed for $6.2 million with John Engel and Melissa Engel of Douglas Elliman, the two-story house is on the market for the first time in 29 years.

Geoffrey Montes is an associate editor at ELLE Decor with a serious love for all things real estate and design. Before that, he worked at Architectural Digest, Galerie, and Preservation magazines, covering everything from jaw-dropping listings to world-famous architects and design events like Salone del Mobile and Homo Faber.

#6630. The Biggest Spring 2027 Trends Celebrate Glamour and Individual Style

Every product on this page was chosen by a Harper's BAZAAR editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Get ready to wear tuxedo pants to brunch and sport a boa for the office

The breakout trends from the Spring 2027 shows were all about personalization and styling, humanizing the process of getting dressed at a time when AI can generate nearly anything for you. From the clashing of statement pieces at Chanel, Dries Van Noten, and Valentino to the coming-out party of the bra at Dior and Altuzarra, the spring runways celebrated self-expression.

There was also a touch of glamour almost everywhere. Dressing for daytime took on a new sheen via the cummerbunds and opera pumps at Kallmeyer, the metallic gold at Saint Laurent, and the boas at Tory Burch. At Michael Rider’s Celine, utilitarian staples were reimagined in sophisticated silhouettes, while brands like C and Bode took classic straight-leg jeans and added embellishment in the form of sparkle and embroidery. This season was all about celebrating the joy of getting dressed, a principle we think should be applied year-round.

And lest we forget, this was the season of the skirt—so much so that Miuccia Prada and Raf Simons sent 63 skirt looks down the Prada runway in Milan. Some were embellished, some had sheer details, and almost all of them fell right below the knee.

Now that fashion month has wrapped and we’ve had some time to digest what we saw on the runways, we’re rounding up our nine favorite trends from the spring 2027 collections. And because March is still months away, we’ve shopped out some ways to get a jump on the looks now.

Mixed-and-matched looks were all over the runways. What felt new here was the against-the-grain pattern play, which felt like a message about personal style—an exhortation from designers to stop worrying about following the rules and lean into whatever makes you feel good.

This approach allows you to play with things that are already in your wardrobe. Invest in a sparkly skirt to pair with a checkered shirt you already own, or pair a new jacquard jacket with some animal-print boots already in your rotation. The key to this trend is making it your own.

Bode Asymmetrical Sequin-Embellished Fringed Skirt

Stripped-back had a whole new meaning this season! Designers quite literally loosened up a few buttons, exposing delicate bras worn underneath shirting. (Granted, some looks like those at Prada and Tom Ford had just a bra as the top, but we’re going to save that one for warmer months.) What’s so appealing about this look is the contrast between the sexy underpinnings and the polished separates. There’s nothing like a little lace to spice things up, and we are here for it!

Borrowing from the boys isn’t new in fashion. Surely I’m not the only one who loves a Winchester banker shirt with monogrammed initials? But the tuxedo dressing trend—which started on the fall 2026 runways and will be going strong all spring—feels fresh. Designers placed a huge emphasis on the waist (I call it “waistmaxxing”), with the focal point of many looks being the cummerbund. A tuxedo jacket, tailored pants, and opera pumps would create a chic head-to-toe approach, but these pieces look just as cool individually. I love tuxedo pants dressed down with a white T-shirt, for example.

We’ve seen neckerchiefs and bandanas trending in previous seasons, but in spring 2027, neckwear takes a decidedly glam turn with the rise of the feather boa. I loved how Tory Burch paired her boas with car coats and silky shirts with trousers, while Dior’s models wore them with extravagant blazers. Both made the case that these three-dimensional scarves should be worn during the day, which feels cool, unexpected, and very much in keeping with the season’s dressed-up turn.

The New Arrivals Ilkyaz Ozel Feather Scarf

Designers really did their duty this season for the uniform dressers out there. If you love a tonal neutral, there were plenty to choose from in updated silhouettes, from balloon-style cargo pants to work shirts with peplums. What made them feel extra special was the way they were accessorized—with bold, colorful heeled boots and a bright satin bag at Celine, or with neon sandal heels at Balenciaga.

JW Anderson Parachute Drawstring Barrel-Leg Track Cargo Pants

The standout jeans this spring are covered in decadent sequins, embroidery, and beading. How fun is that? The key when it comes to styling is keeping your favorite basics on deck and building a look around the jeans. Whether you pair your statement denim with a classic white shirt for work (as seen at Dolce & Gabbana and Bode) or take them for a spin around the town with an equally dazzling top (like at Conner Ives), these are always a good idea—full stop.

Stella McCartney Crystal Embellished Straight-Leg Jeans

Polo Ralph Lauren Embroidered Mid-Rise Straight-Leg Jeans

Ramy Brook Lea Beaded High-Rise Wide-Leg Crop Jeans

With the price of gold at an all-time high and the jewelry market turning to silver, it’s been a refreshing change of pace to see glowing golden hues on the runways. We saw some gold looks sprinkled throughout shows in New York like Ralph Lauren and Fforme, but the collection that really took the medal was Anthony Vaccarello’s final Saint Laurent show, where almost every look had a golden element. From gold jacquard to silky liquid-looking gold tops, these separates can easily spruce up your favorite black trousers for the office or white jeans for a weekend lunch. Or, if you want to take home the trophy, try going head-to-toe gilded for an evening event.

Dries Van Noten Jacquard Linen, Cotton, and Silk-Blend Coat

It’s fair to say that it’s the year of the skirt. Prada may have leaned in hardest, but this classic bottom was all over the runways in every city. The key silhouette was a hybrid pencil/A-line hitting slightly below the knee. (And let me tell you, as a trouser girl, this still sits within my tailored wheelhouse.) There’s a wide range of skirts on the market and an equal range of freedom when it comes to the styling. Whether you pair your skirt with a sweater and loafers for the office or wear it with a sleeveless top and heels for a night out, it’s a piece that can take you just about anywhere.

Massimo Dutti Pleated Bubble Hem Midi Skirt

Polo Ralph Lauren Belted Crinkled Shell-Trimmed Crepe Midi Skirt

Marni Black Crinkled Midi Skirt With Daisy Details

Simone Rocha Pleated Sheer Satin Midi Skirt

Coats aren’t necessarily what most people get excited about in spring—who wants to hide a cute outfit when it’s finally warm enough not to bundle up?—but spring 2027 will be an exception. The coats on the runway this season were much more directional, especially the ones we saw covered in embellishments and jingling and jangling down the catwalk. Next March and April, prepare to layer in pieces that feel equal parts charming and elegant.

Dries Van Noten Embellished Wool Longline Coat

Cinq à Sept Mariella Embellished Faux-Fur-Cuff Coat

Prabal Gurung Floral Jacquard Single-Breasted Car Coat

The “Brainy Heel” Is This Season’s Shoe MVP

My Holiday Wish List Includes One Brand Only

#6628. All About Gabbriette Bechtel, Matty Healy’s Wife

Matty Healy, lead singer of The 1975, has been in a relationship with model and influencer Gabbriette Bechtel since late 2023. In an interview with Highsnobiety published in March 2024, Bechtel candidly shared her feelings regarding the internet’s constant speculation about them.

“I don’t give a shit. I think it’s hilarious. Go for it. Have fun. People have a lot to say,” she laughed. “You don’t know me at all.”

Arguably, Healy’s most well-known relationship came just before he began seeing Bechtel. From May to June 2023, the singer briefly and memorably dated Taylor Swift. The musicians first met in 2014, and while relationship rumors swirled over the years, the two were never official. Swift’s album, The Tortured Poets Department, has even been rumored to be about Healy.

But he and Bechtel have gotten far more serious over their three years of dating and now—marriage! So, who is Healy’s wife? Ahead, everything to know about Bechtel and her relationship with Healy.

Healy and Bechtel’s relationship timeline begins in September 2023. In some photos obtained by People, the pair is seen kissing, hugging, and holding hands while walking together in New York City.

They continued to have PDA sightings, like in November, when they were spotted with their arms wrapped around each other in New York.

Things seem to be getting serious between them by February 2024, when Bechtel was seen spending time with Healy’s family. Photos came out of Healy and Bechtel heading to the theater to watch his mother Denise Welch’s play The Gap. Also in attendance was Healy’s brother, as well as his father, Tim Healy, and his wife.

Previously, in October 2023, Welch showed Bechtel some support on her Instagram. When the model posted a photo wearing nothing but a G-string thong and leather apron from her collaboration with R & M Leathers, Healy’s mom commented, “That’s exactly how I hope to look in mine when it arrives!! 👍👍👍❤️❤️❤️.”

On June 11, the couple announced they were engaged after attending Charli XCX’s Brat Tour show in Brooklyn. Bechtel shared photos of herself wearing the ring, which she was first spotted wearing in May, according to People. The ring has a black diamond in the center that is surrounded by white diamonds.

“MARRYING THE 1975 IS VERY BRAT,” she wrote in the caption.

Denise Welch, Healy’s mom, confirmed the engagement on Loose Women the following day.

“I obviously have known for a few weeks that Matty got engaged,” she said. “They went over to see their friends Charli and George in New York [last night] and I woke up this morning to the fact they’ve put it on Instagram. Instagram official...he’s got engaged to Gabriella Bechtel—Gabbriette she’s known as. Black diamond; he had it made for her...I couldn’t be more thrilled. We couldn’t be happier—she is everything I would want in a daughter-in-law. I’m thrilled.”

The couple was photographed at the O2 Silver Clef Awards on Friday, July 5, sharing a quiet conversation outside Grosvenor House Hotel in London. Bechtel leaned into Healy and kissed him on his cheek. The 1975 frontman was wearing a navy blazer with jeans, and the model wore a black dress with a turtleneck and a pair of black heels. During the ceremony, The 1975 won the Best Group award.

Signaling that they were still going strong in the new year, Bechtel and Healy were spotted on a date in Los Angeles on Jan. 9, 2026. They were photographed walking arm-in-arm to Wilde’s for dinner, a restaurant in Los Feliz. They were both dressed casually in jeans, with Healy wearing a dark cardigan over a white T-shirt and Bechtel in a blazer with rows of gold buttons.

On July 18, Healy and Bechtel were married in Los Angeles at Castillo del Lago, which was formerly owned by Madonna. They appeared kissing at the altar in photos. Bechtel wore a long-sleeve gown with a drop waist and a full floor-length skirt. She had her hair half up with a veil pinned in back. Healy wore a classic black tux and bow tie.

“Matty Healy and Gabbriette Bechtel were married yesterday at a private ceremony in Los Angeles, attended by their family and close friends,” reps for the couple told People.

In an August 2026 interview with Harper’s Bazaar, Bechtel opened up about her impressions of married life with Healy.

“I don’t feel like it’s gonna be very different,” she said. “I feel like I’ve already met my best friend.…The wedding is for our moms and dads. I wanted to do, like, a 50-person thing in a field, so.”

In November 2026, Bechtel and Healy were photographed together in Los Angeles while attending Jessie Jo Stark’s Muse album release celebration. The couple’s outing sparked pregnancy rumors on social media.

Days later, on November 22, People reported that Bechtel and Healy were expecting their first baby together. The news came after the couple was snapped shopping at Bristol Farms in West Hollywood, with Bechtel’s pregnant figure visible in a fitted black dress.

In a May 2023 interview with Vogue, Bechtel shared that she was born and raised in Orange County, and originally moved to Los Angeles to pursue her dream of becoming a professional ballerina.

“I was convinced I was going to be a professional ballerina, and I still am,” she said.

However, her career plans shifted after she was cast by MTV to dance in music videos for Blood Orange (Dev Hynes).

“I put a few dance videos on my Instagram, and somebody reached out to me and said that Dev was having an open call,” she told Highsnobiety. “We had a few fun days of rehearsal and started to film the music video, and then I got a call that I was cut. I didn’t make it into the music video, but I did all of the rehearsals, and I think my feet are in it at some point.”

“I was an unpaid, hard-working model for a very long time,” Bechtel revealed.

Now, Bechtel has modeled for brands like Heaven by Marc Jacobs, Diesel, Miu Miu, and Betsey Johnson. On April 15, 2024, she graced the cover of HommeGirls.

Bechtel is multitalented. In 2019, she was handpicked by Charli XCX to be the lead singer of a band called Nasty Cherry for a Netflix documentary series, I’m with the Band: Nasty Cherry.

“I met [Charli XCX] on the set of a music video, and we ended up partying together for a few years,” she recalls. “I didn’t see her for a while, and then she reached out and was like, ‘I’m doing this project and I’ve been trying to cast the singer…’” Bechtel told Highsnobiety.

Unfortunately, Nasty Cherry is no longer active, with the group choosing to disband during the COVID-19 pandemic.

“We couldn’t do anything together, we couldn’t tour our second EP, and we weren’t able to make music together because we were all in different places in the world. So we just decided that it wasn’t going to work out anymore,” she revealed. “I miss it all the time. It ended too abruptly.”

Bechtel also has a social media presence. Aside from her 1.6 million followers on Instagram, she has amassed 840,400 on TikTok, where she mostly posts cooking videos. In May 2023, she posted a video recreating Erewhon’s grain-free vegan blackout cake, which garnered 3 million views.

Per Highsnobiety, Bechtel shared that she got her love of cooking from her Mexican mother and Swiss-German father, both of whom would cook food from their respective cultures.

“We ate dinner together as a family every single night, so there was always a fresh meal. We never had takeout,” she recalled. “We had a lot of Bon Appétit magazines lying around, so [my mother] would always try new recipes. I would come home and she’d have it open and be like, ‘I’m making this tonight,’ and she’d nail it. I would help her prep, so I grew up with my mom testing out stuff.”

She also shared with Vogue that she is working on a cookbook and eventually would love to open up her own café.

“Not many people know this, but I’m really into interior design. So I’d love to open a space so people can have the full experience,” Bechtel said.

Bechtel was born on July 28, 1997, making her a Leo. Healy, 37, was born on April 8, making him an Aries. According to the AstroTwins, per ELLE.com, “A pair of dynamic fire signs, Aries and Leo make a head-turning duo that whips up passion, adventure, and a heavy dose of drama everywhere you go. A flashy, outspoken pair, you’ll inspire envy. Get ready to reinvent the rules of relationships since your connection is certain to play by its own bold agenda.”

#6627. Years of Being a Private Investigator Taught Me So Much About Dating Men

And it all started with me knowing something was up with “Dirty John” long before he became infamous.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

Years before he became “Dirty John,” an infamous stalker who preyed on a young woman while carrying a butcher knife hidden in a Del Taco bag, I met John Meehan over dinner at the Chateau Marmont. He was dating my friend.

It was 2010, and my friend Kate had recently met a handsome, charming anesthesiologist named John Meehan on a dating website. She was smitten. He was successful, attentive, and apparently desperate to commit. He'd even asked for a key to her house. Kate was head over heels; I was spooked. Something about John didn't add up.

Over dinner, John talked endlessly about his medical career, including the technical details of surgeries with gas ratios and how much money he made. Yet when I asked which medical school he'd attended, he became strangely evasive. When the bill arrived, John meticulously calculated his share down to the penny, despite having spent the evening boasting about his seven-figure income. Maybe he was just frugal. But there was a growing collection of little things that didn't make sense.

Kate thought I was being cynical. At the time, I was an investigative journalist and former sex columnist living in L.A. who had recently enrolled in private investigator school. So, sure, maybe that wasn’t too misplaced. But I trust my instincts and genuinely thought she was in danger—when John left the restaurant, I borrowed my then-husband's Mercedes and followed him. John had told Kate he lived with his sister in Pasadena. He drove somewhere else entirely.

Over the following days, I began digging. I discovered that John wasn't the doctor he claimed to be—he was a nurse anesthetist with a long criminal history, had lost his professional license, and had been accused of stalking women.

I had mixed feelings about telling Kate the truth. I knew that I had to break the bad news, but I was super nervous when I showed up to our happy hour date carrying a printout of John’s mug shot. Fortunately, she believed me and broke things off. And although I suspected at the time that he was a con artist with a serious capacity for violence, I was left feeling helpless.

Years later, I listened in horror as the Dirty John podcast chronicled what happened when John Meehan entered his wife Debra Newell’s life. Their story mirrored his and Kate’s, except that when she tried to break up with him, things took a much darker turn. He eventually attacked her adult daughter, Terra Newell, who bravely fought back and killed him in self-defense. Hearing that, I was relieved that Kate had escaped. But I was also haunted by the knowledge that recognizing a dangerous man hadn't stopped him from finding another victim.

From sitting outside no-tell motels on matrimonial surveillance to piecing together a crime victim’s last day on earth, I’ve seen many situations where people get into dangerous situations—from confronting love rivals to going back for one last suitcase—all for love.

Here’s what detective work has taught me about dating:

Before I became a private investigator, I was a sex and dating columnist for The Independent in London. I spent years documenting my romantic adventures, writing about everything from casual sex to heartbreak. I've always been fascinated by the similarities between dating and detective work.

In both instances, you’re sitting across from a relative stranger. You’re asking questions, and you’re trying to figure out whether they are telling the truth—all the while hoping that, in the dating realm, any skeletons you unearth are metaphorical, and not decomposing in someone’s crawlspace.

But there's one enormous difference. When I'm investigating someone professionally, I'm looking for inconsistencies. When I'm falling for someone, I'm looking for reasons to believe them. And that's where things get dangerous.

It’s been scientifically proven that love causes a dopamine reaction in the brain similar to eating pounds of chocolate or snorting cocaine. Think about it: Would you make any other major life decision—like who to live with, introduce to your children or combine finances with—while high? That’s why I love the idea of a “sexual sober friend,” aka someone who will tell you straight up if you are being totally irrational. Of course, you have to actually be willing to take said friend’s advice–see below!

One of the most useful lessons I learned in PI school was to not to “give people the benefit of the doubt.” Instead, we say, “Trust, but verify.”

When you're investigating a crime, you build a timeline. You figure out what happened, when it happened, and whether the evidence supports the accounts you've been given. I think romantic relationships benefit from a much less obsessive version of the same principle.

Someone tells you they're looking for a serious relationship. Do their actions support that statement? Do they make plans? Follow through? Introduce you to people in their lives? Respect your boundaries? Or do they deliver a spectacular speech about wanting to find their soulmate and then disappear for eleven days because, apparently, their phone fell into the sea? A charming explanation is not the same thing as a credible one.

I once worked on a surveillance case involving a wife whose husband worked a boring day job as a dentist...but she was never allowed to come to his office, and he would disappear for hours in the evening. When he told her that he wanted a divorce and was leaving, she wanted to know where he was going, so she hired me and my partner.

After 18 hours of surveillance, I was able to confirm what six years of couples’ therapy had failed to deliver: confirmation that not only was he cheating on her with his receptionist, which I captured on camera while hiding in a deserted parking lot, but that he was leading a double life as a wannabee-Soprano small-time strip club drug dealer.

The next day, as I played the video evidence of his double life, writing prescriptions for several dancers, smoking pot, and shopping the aisles of Trader Joe’s for munchies, she told me that she had suspected for years that something was off, but she never felt ready to ask him the questions that would shatter her world. That’s happened to me a lot when I was doing matrimonial surveillance: People will tell me about finding incriminating photographs, suspicious messages, or underwear that clearly doesn't belong to them—and then ask me whether I think something is wrong.

Often, they're not really asking me to uncover the truth. They're asking me to give them permission to believe what they've already seen. Which brings me to my next principle:

I believe in intuition, but I don’t believe that it’s supernatural magic. Sometimes it's simply our brains recognizing patterns before we've consciously identified them.

At that first dinner with John Meehan, I couldn't immediately explain why I felt uncomfortable. But as the evening continued, I used my investigative training to spot the inconsistencies: his evasiveness, his elaborate claims, the mismatch between the person he presented and the details of his life. The important part wasn't that I had a bad feeling, it was that I paid attention to the feeling and then looked for facts.

Even after years of studying human behavior, I still sometimes find myself making the same mistakes I warn other people about.

While working as a journalist on a murder investigation in Arkansas, I became attracted to someone I was interviewing, someone who had been good friends with a possible person of interest. I knew perfectly well that personal chemistry could interfere with my judgment but found myself getting involved anyway.

Investigative work can teach you to be skeptical, but skepticism isn't a force field. You can know that someone is wrong for you and still want them. And you can be exceptionally good at uncovering other people's secrets while remaining spectacularly talented at hiding things from yourself.

A lot of people expect me to turn into Nancy Drew with every man I meet. There’s some truth to that; like most women, I do a Google deep dive before meeting someone for the first time.

But after spending years investigating cheating spouses, con artists, and eventually a cold-case murder, I've become convinced that there's a fine line between healthy curiosity and turning your love life into a full-time surveillance operation.

I don't think women should spend their evenings running background checks on every man who buys them a cocktail. Nor should they secretly follow their partners or interpret every unanswered text as evidence of a double life.

For years, I thought being a good investigator meant finding answers to every question. Now I think one of the most important skills—both in detective work and in dating—is knowing when you have enough information to make a decision. And when it's time to stop investigating and get on with your life.

This image is of a professional model and used for illustrative purposes only.

Catherine Townsend is an investigative journalist, licensed private investigator, and host of the true-crime podcast Hell and Gone. Her new book, HOW TO SURVIVE A MURDER INVESTIGATION: A True Story of Death and Danger in the Ozarks, is out now.

#6625. Van Cleef & Arpels Explores the Secrets and Mysteries of Ancient Egypt

The maison’s latest high jewelry collection taps into the mystique of the Valley of the Kings

After archaeologist Howard Carter and his team discovered King Tutankhamun’s tomb in 1922, regal diadems, golden collars, and bold cuffs brought a new wave of Egyptian Revival into the Art Deco movement. Van Cleef & Arpels took note and added Egyptian-inspired calligraphy, hieroglyphs, and semiprecious stones into its lexicon of deco bracelets, sautoirs, and brooches, bringing a historic feel to the frostbitten glamour that defined the roaring ’20s. Now, over a century later, the maison’s new Fascinating Egypt high jewelry collection explores more avenues of the region’s mystical splendor.

From opulent collars to sculptural rings, each design harkens back to the arts, values, and beliefs that defined the New Kingdom. But just as Egyptologists explored hidden messages beneath the grandeur of the 18th dynasty, Van Cleef conveys the mystique beneath its history. Shape-shifting necklaces function like secret passageways to new realms of expression, and Mysterioux brooches reincarnate sacred deities in gold and gemstones. Just take the Bennou, a terrestrial vessel for the sun god Ra, whose feathers of emeralds, marine-blue sapphires, lapis, cerulean slips of turquoise, mother of pearl, and drops of diamonds represent the colors of the earth. A blood-orange spessartite garnet with rays of diamonds hangs from a red spinel, like a drop of sunshine.

“There are a lot of real animals that were fashioned into amulets and small statues, likely to imbue the owners with some of their traits, but also to revere the creature and the benefits it provided,” archaeologist Randall Hixenbaugh tells Bazaar. His namesake gallery in Manhattan’s Design District is home to artifacts sourced in accordance with the ethical standards set by the International Association of Dealers in Ancient Arts.

Talismans were worn like supplements to enhance the human experience—cats to spark joy, mongooses to offer protection from snakes, and frogs for fertility. Stones of certain colors were valued for their connotations with abundance and immortality. “The Egyptians prized bright blue and green materials, which were associated with the Nile, the sky, and life itself. Lapis lazuli was rare and valuable and therefore associated with the pharaohs. Its extensive use on King Tut’s gold mask shows how highly regarded it was,” Hixenbaugh explains.

The cobalt stone finds its way into all kinds of Van Cleef’s latest creations. It’s used generously in the “Ornement de Saphir” necklace, whose central totem resembles an evil eye, with an oval-cut sapphire pupil plopped cleverly in an iris of pavé diamonds. Suspended from a panel of lapis lazuli interspersed with slivers of gold and baguette-cut rubies, the pendant is detachable and can be worn like a brooch, while its Corde chain incorporates clasps concealed beneath colorful rondelles that allow it to transform into a pair of bracelets. A concept conceived in the 1940s, the versatile signature speaks to the ongoing conversation between the house’s heritage and Egypt’s mystique.

Some of Van Cleef’s most striking interpretations of the ancient kingdom come through in Art Deco bracelets. The articulated cuff style was a staple of the 1920s, with ample real estate for planes of illustrious motifs to flourish. Instead of a painter’s palette, Van Cleef brought diamonds, emeralds, and rubies together like dots on a pointillist painting through the “mystery set” technique (a manner of setting gemstones sans prongs).

The house patented the method in the 1930s and has continued to enlist it to craft seamless gemscapes, extending into a series of “Paysage” bracelets. Clad in fields of gemstones, the deco-style pieces bring Egyptian royals, celestial skies, and monuments to life. Openwork, a technique that minimizes the use of solid metals, enables light to pass through each bejeweled mural, amplifying the high shine of each cuff. Other inconspicuous elements, like golden hieroglyphic nuggets, rest beneath articulated columns, like artifacts awaiting discovery.

The house’s bold fonts of design can be equally understood through the juxtaposition of lolly-like cabochons and sugar-white diamonds. Amid the sea of platinum and flashy Asscher cuts that defined Gatsby-era decadence, Van Cleef & Arpels brought teardrop emeralds into the mix. Suspended from pillars of diamonds set in platinum, the plump stones hung like enticing lozenges throughout a collaret necklace, circa 1929. The spellbinding masterpiece drew the eye of Egyptian royalty, Her Royal Highness Princess Faiza, who purchased the necklace in 1937. It was a convergence of brilliant facets and vivid, dome-shaped gems that bridged the gradual shift into midcentury modern shapes and matte stones. The provenance of the design influenced the maison’s updated rendition of the crown jewel, featuring luscious emeralds and slender pearls suspended from elongated pendants of white gold and diamonds.

By the 1950s, Van Cleef’s interpretations of Egyptomania embraced organic forms, space-age ellipses, and plots of semiprecious stones. Smooth, matte surfaces of turquoise, malachite, and coral enhanced the realism of biomorphic designs, like a scarab clip with wings of turquoise, circa 1957. (The house would lend the brooch to the Petit Palais for the debut of “Tutankhamun and His Time” nearly a decade later.) Such vibrant stones extended into contemporary aesthetics of the 1980s, such as a collar necklace slathered in slices of jade, stripes of onyx, and birds carved out of coral.

This collection revisited the era through the language of Ettore Sottsass and the Memphis Group, a 1980s decorative arts firm, for spherical shapes and elements of cubism to take flight. Some of Sottsass’s most recognizable pieces include a sterling silver fruit stand with zigzag pillars and a colorblocked bookstand named “The Carlton.” Van Cleef translates this friction between elements smooth and sharp, glossy and matte into pieces like a saucer-shaped Mythe Celeste clip, coated in sheets of lapis and malachite with buttons of coral—elements beheld by the Egyptians as modalities of abundance and immortality. By contrast, a Frise Divine ring juxtaposes an oval-shaped Sri Lankan sapphire with a rigid tile of coral, and rows of diamonds set in zigzag streams of gold.

All creations in the Fascinating Egypt œuvre are united by tradition, a preservation of heritage echoed in the foundations of other art forms, like ballet. In fact, the house’s seamless dances of emeralds, rubies, and diamonds compelled George Balanchine to choreograph Jewels for the New York City Ballet in 1931—a moment that came full circle when the jewelers debuted a collection of ballerina clips and brooches a couple of years later. The nature of their anatomically sound arms, gilded in gold and ribbons of gemstones, extends into the collection’s Muse Éternelle brooches. Modeled after Cleopatra, the palm-sized figures are enrobed in a variety of dazzling threads of gold, strewn with diamonds, sapphires, rubies, and emeralds.

#6624. Buck Mason Just Released a Very Cozy Collab with Sierra Designs

It’s the first team-up between the much-loved menswear brand and iconic outdoor supplier.

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

El Niño be damned. Winter is just around the corner, and even in a very weird year for weather, I can guarantee you this: There are cold days ahead. It pays to be prepared for them. And you could do a whole lot worse, preparation-wise, than the new collaboration between Buck Mason and Sierra Designs.

You probably know both brands already, but just in case you don’t, here’s a brief primer. Buck Mason, founded in 2013, makes the kind of cool-but-accessible menswear staples that you can build an entire wardrobe around. Sierra Designs is one of the OG American outdoors brands, founded in 1965 and responsible for iconic designs like the 60/40 mountain parka and all sorts of other equipment and clothing built for exploring nature. Both, handily enough, were born in California—and today they released their first-ever collaboration.

The new collection draws heavily from Sierra Designs’ ‘70s heyday, with reworked versions of an archival down jacket and vest serving as the stars of the show. Both are made from lightweight Japanese nylon and utilize a 600-fill-power, 80/20 blend of down and feathers for insulation. They’re complemented by a range of vintage-y tees, thermals, totes, and caps.

The overall vibe is easygoing and not too techy—exactly the sort of stuff you’ll want to wear in everyday life, not just on some windswept summit. Which is what makes it so damn good. There’s no shortage of high-performance winter gear out there, but much of it falls short on the style front. This is the sort of stuff that’ll do the job but won’t have you looking like you just finished a black-diamond run. Wear it with chinos and hiking boots or jeans and sneakers. To the coffee shop on a blustery morning or the park for a day out with the family. Or, yeah, even in the great outdoors. Just make sure to snag whatever you like soon, as Buck Mason’s collaborations have a nasty habit of selling out.
