#6593. Fashion Has Moved Beyond the Runway and Into the Director’s Chair

A new generation of luxury houses is stepping behind (and beyond) the camera.

Ahead of Matthieu Blazy’s first Chanel Métiers d’art show, Michel Gondry introduced the collection’s world through a surreal short starring Margaret Qualley and A$AP Rocky. Released as a prelude to the runway, the film follows the couple as they drift through New York along parallel paths before finding each other again on a downtown sidewalk, where Rocky proposes moments before they disappear into the subway station together.

When guests, including Rocky and Qualley, arrived at the show, the story continued where the film had left off. Bhavitha Mandava emerged in silk “jeans,” followed later by Mona Tougaard, Anok Yai, and Alex Consani, each embodying a different New York archetype, from the sharply tailored power executive to the impeccably dressed society woman. As they moved through the decommissioned station at Bowery and Kenmare Street in Blazy’s reimagined tweeds, the everyday commute took on a cinematic quality. “Designers are directors themselves, except we’re all their actors,” says Jane Rosenthal, an Academy Award-nominated producer, co-founder of the Tribeca Festival, and co-chair of the board of Tribeca Enterprises. “When they create characters this vivid, they’re creating not just a dress, but a feeling people want to inhabit.”

Fashion’s love affair with the silver screen stretches across decades. Coco Chanel designed costumes for two Jean Renoir films; Hubert de Givenchy created Audrey Hepburn’s black sheath dress for Breakfast at Tiffany’s. Today, the relationship runs in both directions. Julian Klausner’s spring/summer 2027 collection for Dries Van Noten borrowed the language of cinema through imagined movie posters splashed on slips and party skirts, while the Angelina Jolie-starring Couture turned its attention to the women behind the seams.

And as film culture enjoys a renaissance of its own, fashion is deepening its relationship with the medium. Letterboxd has turned cinephilia into a social, gamified experience, while celebrities now revel in the chance to highlight their most obscure Criterion Collection favorites. Fashion, ever attuned to shifts in culture, has followed suit, moving beyond the familiar fields of costume design and cinematic campaigns to support and sometimes produce filmmaking itself.

Since 2015, Through Her Lens: The Tribeca Chanel Women’s Filmmaker Program, co-founded by Rosenthal, has paired mentorship with financial support for emerging female and nonbinary filmmakers. This year’s program brought together mentors such as Celine Song and Gina Prince-Bythewood, who worked directly with participants.

Rosenthal points out that the number of female-directed films has, unfortunately, dipped in recent years. After over a decade of the program, “What we’re finding is that the community is really intentional,” she says. “Our advisory board wants to be here. They come back year after year. They want to be part of it, because they want to help support the next generation.” Miu Miu’s Women’s Tales, established by Miuccia Prada in 2011, has similarly become a platform for female filmmakers—including Ava DuVernay, Claire Denis, and Mona Fastvold—to tell original stories. Those initiatives were early signs of a broader shift.

In September, Dior announced a two-year creative partnership with A24, the studio behind auteur-driven hits like Marty Supreme and Everything Everywhere All at Once, spanning film, theater, and live events at the Cherry Lane Theatre in Manhattan’s West Village. The collaboration was introduced online with a tongue-in-cheek short directed by Aidan Zamiri, in which Sabrina Carpenter, Josh O’Connor, and Drew Starkey auditioned to play Dior creative director Jonathan Anderson. (The announcement kicked off with a companion teaser, featuring Carpenter dressed as Anderson himself, complete with his now instantly recognizable formula of a plaid shirt, jeans, and Salomons.)

Under its former creative director Anthony Vaccarello, Saint Laurent launched Saint Laurent Productions in 2023, backing acclaimed films including Pedro Almodóvar’s Strange Way of Life and Jacques Audiard’s Oscar-winning melodrama Emilia Pérez. And Rolex has also invested in cinema’s future, supporting the Student Academy Awards, which platform emerging filmmakers, partnering with festivals like the New York Film Festival and the Sundance Institute as an official partner and exclusive timepiece, and serving as a founding supporter of the Academy Museum of Motion Pictures.

Tara Maria Gonzalez, a fashion writer and author of the Substack newsletter Cult Classic, notes that film allows brands room to build worlds rather than simply present collections or have their designs live in the endless scroll of social media. “People are growing tired of seeing the same trends,” she explains. “It’s like, ‘Well, why do I need to buy that if everybody else has it?’ When designers align themselves with directors, that adds a level of depth that can also connect to a wider audience.”

Look no further than Balenciaga, which enlisted Past Lives and Materialists director Song to helm the short film A New York Minute. Song built a character study around Sarah Pidgeon, using Pierpaolo Piccioli’s designs as a narrative thread rather than the main attraction. It leaves the viewer to focus on a mood before a product, an inversion of what fashion advertising has traditionally asked us to do. “Brands are [showing] that fashion isn’t just for people who self-identify as ‘fashion people,’” Gonzalez notes. “Fashion has the mass appeal that can speak to anybody who’s interested in storytelling. And one of the most accessible forms of storytellingis film.”

Like Gondry’s lovers disappearing into the subway station, the most memorable fashion stories don’t end when the collection arrives on the runway or on the shop floor. They only become more interesting out on the streets, where clothes become characters and the city becomes the set.

#6591. Police Make Arrests After Prince William and Kate’s Norfolk Home Is Broken Into

Prince William and Princess Catherine suffered a security scare on Monday, as their Anmer Hall country home in Norfolk, England, was broken into.

At around 6 p.m. local time, police were called to the residence with a report of suspicious activity, Norfolk Constabulary said in a statement obtained by People. Since then, two men have been arrested on suspicion of burglary at the address.

Per a report from The Times, police are investigating allegations that the masked intruders successfully entered the property.

The Prince and Princess of Wales, and their three children—Prince George, 13, Princess Charlotte, 11, and Prince Louis, 8—were not in the residence at the time of the break-in. The family is believed to have been in Forest Lodge, their home base in Windsor, at the time.

“Officers attended and specialist resources, including police dogs, drones and the National Police Air Service helicopter, were deployed to assist with searches in the area,” Norfolk Constabulary said.

The 10-bedroom royal residence is located on King Charles III’s private Sandringham estate, and was given by Queen Elizabeth II to William and Kate as a wedding gift following their 2011 ceremony.

The incident comes two years after two masked people broke into the Windsor Castle estate as William, Kate, and their kids slept at home. The burglars did not attempt to enter the Wales family’s Adelaide Cottage, but rather broke into Shaw Farm, one of the working farms inside the estate. The robbers got past armed security at the property gate, and stole farm machinery. The royals were not harmed.

#6590. Revisiting Julianne Hough’s Dating History Amid Rumors She and Adrian Williams Have Possibly Already Split

The concept of her attending the Met Gala with Ryan Seacrest… twice.

Julianne Hough has graced our screens for nearly two decades thanks to her ties to Dancing With the Stars and projects like Safe Haven and Burlesque. For someone who’s been in the entertainment biz for a minute, Julianne has surprisingly kept her personal life under wraps. Well, for the most part.

Aside from her music, dancing skills, and red carpet looks, the media personality has also made headlines for her rumored romances and high-profile relationships. (Because who could forget she dated Ryan Seacrest back in the day? Certainly not this writer who lived through it.)

Anyway! As she interviews celebs in the DWTS skybox and joins them for numbers on the dance floor, you might be wondering who she’s romantically linked to these days. The latest development: She and Peloton instructor Adrian Williams are reportedly already over...even though news of their rumored relationship broke less than 24 hours ago.

Ahead, let’s revisit Julianne Hough’s full dating history, including some surprise rumors.

Before Julianne moved out to L.A. to be a pro on DWTS’s early seasons, she was romantically linked to her childhood sweetheart, Zack Wilson, who she had known since she was 9.

“I was engaged to someone else, a guy I knew from Utah who had served a Mormon mission,” she said during a 2012 interview with Glamour, adding that he was “a great guy.”

They ended up calling off their engagement in November 2007, and according to Julianne, it fizzled out because she was ~soul-searching.~

“I needed to figure myself out more, and so I moved to L.A. and we ended it,” she shared, adding that he went on to marry and have a kid with her childhood best friend.

“It was a little weird when it happened, but they’re a great couple!” she admitted.

Okay, so her engagement didn’t work out—but it led to her first high-profile relationship with country singer Chuck Wicks after they met on Brad Paisley’s tour in August 2008.

It was truly a whirlwind romance, since he publicly declared they were “very much in love” on red carpets and even gave her a birthday shoutout on stage. By December that year, they had apparently discussed potentially getting married.

Of course, the pair walked many a red carpet and even became partners on DWTS in 2009.

They had reportedly had a “mutual” split by November 2009, and though things seemed amicable at the time, Chuck admitted that “shit hit the fan” nearly a decade later.

“I’m not going to throw anybody under the bus but … it wasn’t my fault,” he said on his Talk to Chuck podcast in 2020, adding that he “played the game” and kept things civil in public, though he “didn’t really like her at the time.”

“Now, I just don't even care,” he admitted. “It’s on you. You get past it. I’ve seen her one time. I’ve seen her one time in 10 years.”

There’s not much to go on here, since the Safe Haven actor and comedian never confirmed whether they were an item. However, in March 2010, a source told People that Dane had “kissed her, and they quickly left together” after he headlined a comedy show.

They were seen together in West Hollywood one more time before moving on.

Though Julianne claimed a friend tried to set her up with the American Idol host when she was still engaged to Zack, she didn’t give him a chance until things ended with Chuck years later. During the aforementioned Glamour interview, she revealed she had properly met Ryan when she and Chuck appeared on his radio show as a couple.

“I blush when I hear [the interview] because Ryan is hitting on me the entire time!” she shared. “At the end, Ryan said something like, ‘You guys really have something special!’ And I thought to myself, ‘He’s so full of shit right now!’ When Chuck and I broke up, I wanted to be single. That lasted five months. Then Ryan and I went on a date.”

Her interview with Chuck was seemingly scrubbed from the internet (womp), but she appeared on Ryan’s radio show many times after. This was pretty much the vibe every time:

Anyway, their one date eventually turned into a three-year relationship, complete with Golden Globes, Emmys, and two Met Gala appearances.

According to People’s sources, they eventually split in March 2013 because of “busy work schedules,” though they’re still cool to this day.

When asked whether it was awkward for the exes to chat on-record during a joint interview with her brother, Derek Hough, in 2022, Ryan said, “There is nothing uncomfortable about it because we remained friends for years and still are.”

Julianne maintained that they still had “respect and love” for each other after their split.

After her split with Ryan, Julianne was reportedly linked to Oscar-winning filmmaker Ari Sandel in July 2013 when they were spotted making out at Nobu.

In August 2013, they were photographed leaving a party at the Chateau Marmont and grabbed brunch in West Hollywood the next day. They never publicly commented on their relationship, so it’s unclear how long they actually dated, if at all.

This is Julianne’s biggest and most public relationship to date. She met former hockey star Brooks Laich through mutual friends before they started a long-distance relationship in 2013. (He lived in Washington, D.C., while Julianne was still based in L.A.)

They eventually got engaged in 2015 and tied the knot at an outdoor ceremony on Idaho’s Lake Coeur d’Alene in 2017. However, their romance would come to an end by May 2020, when they announced their split in a joint statement to People.

“We have lovingly and carefully taken the time we have needed to arrive at our decision to separate. We share an abundance of love and respect for one another and will continue to lead with our hearts from that place,” they said at the time.

During an August 2024 appearance on Dax Shepard’s Armchair Expert podcast, Julianne admitted that she and Brooks were “sad that it didn’t work out” between them—especially since he’s remained close with her brother, Derek.

“I feel like our relationship and our marriage was exactly right, and the end of it is right, too,” she explained at the time. “We both have regrets that it didn’t work out because I don’t think we had the maturity to come together. He was contracting while I was expanding. We just couldn’t find each other in that.”

Back in December 2024, Julianne was photographed after a dinner date with Bachelor Nation alum Tanner Courtad. That was the only time we had seen anything about the pair…until Tanner fully spilled the tea during the Bachelor Happy Hour podcast in November 2026.

“That was short-lived, but it was fun. I was going to Dancing With the Stars to support Joey [Graziadei], and I was sitting in the front row, and Ezra [Sosa] came over to me, and he goes, ‘Hey, our host thinks you’re cute. Can I give you her number?’” he shared.

He continued, “I actually met her out in the parking lot. We talked for a couple of minutes, decided to go on a date and talked for a little bit. We went to the park with her dog, got some sushi, hung out for the night and that’s when we got spotted by the paparazzi is picking up sushi.”

Though they never kept seeing each other, Tanner confirmed, “It was very short-lived. Julianne’s great, but it was never anything more than just, like, a singular date.”

On November 23—two months after Julianne cryptically revealed that she “might not be single”—news broke that Julianne was romancing Peloton instructor Adrian Williams. Per TMZ, the two met over the summer “through a mutual friend in the wellness community.”

Then, less than 24 hours later, TMZ broke the news that Julianne “briefly dated Adrian, but they are no longer together,” adding, “It's unclear what caused the breakup or who ended the relationship…but it happened recently because the two were seen out together on Sunday.”

That’s all for now, but we’re actively rooting for Julianne and her hot girl agenda.

#6588. The 20 Best Colognes to Give as Holiday Gifts

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

Impress everyone on your list with these great scents.

Fragrance makes a fantastic holiday gift. It’s something they can wear every day, a good bottle lasts for ages, and it feels undeniably luxurious. The only catch? Dropping serious cash on a scent they don’t actually like would, frankly, suck. Luckily, we’re pretty obsessed with fragrance here at Esquire. We’ve smelled, tested, and worn more colognes than we can count—and we know which ones tend to be crowd-pleasers.

It helps to know what your giftee is into. Do they want something light and fresh they can wear year-round? Something dark and smoky for winter? Or are they new to cologne, meaning a beloved classic is the safest bet? (And we know the fan favorites by heart—as well as all the best new fragrances from the past year.) Whatever their taste (or level of fragrance expertise), we put our heads—and noses—together to find the best colognes to gift everyone on your list.

It’s just sexy. This fragrance is not one to go under the radar and escape people’s attention. From experience, it will draw people in from across the room to ask what you’re wearing. A little clichéd but very much tried and tested. It’s unabashedly smoky and resinous, which makes for a captivating cloud of mystery. —Alfonso Fernández Navas, senior style and market editor

It’s a classic for a reason. Aromatic and bold. I like to wear it for the important kind of evenings when I need an extra bit of refinement. —Mark Burger, social-media editor

Fulton & Roark makes so many delicious fragrances, but Ramble is one of my favorites. It’s inspired by the famous Central Park enclave of the same name, so it’s perfect for any NYC native. The notes of forest air, lavender, and lemon rind create a fresh, woody scent that will transport you to the wilderness. It’s sophisticated and lush, and it works for literally everyone. —Bellamy Richardson, associate commerce editor

A reliable fastball of a men’s going-out scent. Up top, it’s fresh and gets compliments without being an attention-grabbing club citrus fragrance. On the dry down, it’s deep and sophisticated without being too fashion-y and cerebral.

This would be my go-to for most guys. I’ve given bottles to my brother (divorced dad in his 40s) and his oldest son (just turned 21). Gets compliments in both age ranges. —Luke Guillory, commerce editor

A versatile scent that wears well practically any time of the year. The citrusy notes are potent but not overwhelming. You need only two sprays maximum for this to hit. —Eric Francisco, entertainment editor

Snif’s new Double Take cologne is one of our new favorite scents—and at $74, it’s a much more affordable option than some of the more luxury picks on this list. With juicy apricot and fresh orange blossom at the top, creamy sesame milk at the heart, and a classic woody base, it’s beloved by everyone in our office who’s tried it. —B.R.

After years of using Ralph Lauren Polo Gold Blend, my wife took me cologne shopping at Le Labo in the West Village, where we spent more than an hour trying out different fragrances. I ended up going with Baie 19, which brings an earthy and refreshing feeling. It paired well with my body smell, which is a huge factor. Le Labo also offers a huge discount with its refill program; you just have to bring your bottle back. —Mike Kim, digital design director

Woody, rosy, fresh—a bright and sophisticated scent I like to wear all day. Plus, if it’s good enough for Tom Holland, it’s good enough for me. —M.B.

Byredo Mojave Ghost smells effortless, and even my friends who don’t usually like scents always compliment it. The bottle design also makes it perfect to put on display—it looks so chic sitting on a desk or counter! —Emma Saxon, fashion assistant

Ranger Station is a little perfumer out of Nashville, and as such it’s got a lot of country-music references in the lineup. This one, done with the Waylon Jennings estate, embodies the man himself: gruff, masculine, old-fashioned.

It’s a curveball as a gift because it’s heavy on musk, leather, smoke, and spice. It smells like the Stetson Original my grandpa used. (It smells more like it than the modern Stetson Original does, funnily enough.) But that’s why I love the idea. This type of scent has been lost to time, so these days it’s really, really unique. —L.G.

If you know someone who can’t live without coffee, they’ll probably love a coffee-scented cologne. This one from Maison Margiela’s Replica line is one of our favorites thanks to its nice blend of sweet and spicy notes. It also strikes a perfect balance of light and fresh (from top notes of lemon, lavender, and spearmint) and rich gourmand (from notes of warm coffee, milk mousse, and sweet vanilla), so it’s not too heavy in either direction. —B.R.

Pretty much any Dolce & Gabbana cologne is a winner in our books, but The One for Men collection is a good bet for any guy. The eau de parfum is a woody, ambery fragrance centered around orange, sage, and tobacco, giving it a sophisticated overall vibe that works well at the office or on a date. —B.R.

I gravitate toward gifting warm, cozy scents and this one feels perfectly timed for the season. With notes of almond, rose, and vanilla, this powdery gourmand feels soft and intimate. A touch of honey adds some nostalgic sweetness. —Andrea Ríos, fashion assistant

This is a new favorite of mine I’ve been wearing every day. Iris Germanica and vanilla give it a floral sweetness that takes the edge of the manly musk typical of Legend. Plus, it’s hard to go wrong with anything served in a flask. —Bryn Gelbart, associate commerce editor

I really campaigned for this scent during our Grooming Awards earlier this year. It’s insanely wearable. Spice, florals, and citrus top and dry down hit in the warmer months. In the colder months, the warm, earthy heart stands out. It’s soft without being too femme, sturdy without the usual smoke and whiskey smells. I think the difficulty with this fragrance would be finding someone who it doesn’t jive with. —L.G.

Iris Silver Mist by Serge Lutens was first introduced in 1994, and there’s a reason this unisex scent remains so coveted. This fragrance blends the scent of moist, green earth with the light, powdery, and slightly metallic qualities of iris. Let this be your introduction to niche perfumery. —E.S.

If you know anyone who likes warm scents for the colder months, this new release might just be perfect. The notes of cognac and antique wood make me feel as though I’m sitting in an old home next to a roaring fire with a drink in hand. But it’s not overwhelming or cloying—just inviting. —Jonathan Evans, style director

I don’t wear scents on a daily basis, but whenever I spritz one on for a special occasion, it’s always Diptyque. The brand makes the most beautiful yet understated fragrances that are just right, never leaving too heavy of a cast or going unnoticeable. Lazulio is my latest go-to, a more natural scent that is a perfect blend of fresh and classic in a single bottle.

A gift from the brand always stands out thanks to its beautiful packaging. No matter what, the recipient will feel the luxury. Plus, I can bet they don’t have this scent yet. But if you’re not exactly sure what they like, you can play it safe with this discovery set of five scents. —Krista Jones, senior director, commerce and partnerships

One of my favorite scents. Versatile enough to move from day to night yet striking enough to leave an impression no matter the hour. The balance of bergamot, musk, neroli, and vetiver is masterful. The bottle—with tinges of Art Deco influence—is equally sharp. —M.B.

Aside from the grenade casing being so much fun to use, Viktor Rolf’s Spicebomb really booms with its strong combination of pepper and cinnamon. Wears well during the colder seasons and for more formal occasions (despite its aggressive appearance). —E.F.

#6587. Teyana Taylor’s Deconstructed F1 Jacket Was Previously Worn on the Race Track

Someone get Teyana Taylor on the race track. This weekend, the “Gonna Love Me” artist headed to Las Vegas for the Formula 1 Grand Prix—and she showed up looking ready to race, thanks to an on-theme deconstructed leather jacket from Alpinestars.

The Alpinestars label, best known for its motorsport equipment, has been creating protective essentials for racers for over 60 years now. With its new RSRV line, however, the company has expanded into ready-to-wear—blurring the lines between performance gear and everyday statement pieces.

For its Spring/Summer 2027 collection, Structures of Speed, this meant creating eight leather jackets made from authentic MotoGP crash suits that were actually worn by riders. The Crashed Leather Jackets, as they’re called, preserved real scuffs and signs of wear, and came with documents identifying the original riders.

Taylor’s piece featured a black base with blue patchwork, red details, and zipper accents. It carried her from the Ferrari garage—where she met up with All’s Fair costar-slash-F1 first lady Kim Kardashian, whose boyfriend, Lewis Hamilton, drives for the Scuderia Ferrari team—to the Raising Cane’s suite to watch the qualifying race, and finally to a SkinCeuticals activation.

The singer paired her boxy jacket with a black tee embellished with a red-and-orange flame motif. She grabbed oversize sunglasses, and for shoes, she went with thigh-high leather boots from Balenciaga—a house that has previously collaborated directly with Alpinestars. In addition to Kardashian—who wore Ferrari red for the occasion—Taylor was joined by the likes of Nina Dobrev and Adam Sandler.

#6583. Tom Hardy's 5 Most Stylish Characters, Ranked

From Inception's sharply-dressed con-man to the getaway driver in RocknRolla, here’s what you can learn from his on-screen wardrobe.

Tom Hardy doesn’t usually play the good guy. He’s been a forger, a fixer, a getaway driver, a rogue spy, and, in one of his more ambitious films, both halves of the most notorious twins in British crime (not that there’s much competition). Most of them are walking red flags, sure, but they’re red flags in great clothes.

That’s partly down to the costume designers, who’ve given Hardy everything from ’60s East End tailoring to Steve McQueen-inspired ’70s outerwear, but it’s also down to Hardy, who has a particular knack for making a wardrobe feel like his own. Put him in a Western shirt or three-piece and he’ll look equally at home in both (and you’ll want to steal the look, either way).

So, we’ve dug through the archives to rank his best-dressed roles of all time, and one of them’s actually on TV right now. Consider it your new watch list—just ignore the red flags.

Everyone remembers the spinning top and Joseph Gordon-Levitt’s three-piece, but the best-dressed man in Inception is the one who usually gets overlooked: Tom Hardy’s Eames. Costume designer Jeffrey Kurland imagined him as an expat from a Graham Greene novel, and every piece was made from scratch (even though half of it looks straight off a Dries Van Noten runway). Think batik-print shirts, collars spread over relaxed blazers, sharp trousers, and a wallet chain—basically clothes for a guy who spends too long sitting in the sun and definitely doesn’t wear SPF. Then there’s the watch: a gold tank-style antique Kurland picked up from the prop master’s collection—and he’s since forgotten the brand. Reddit’s watch sleuths are still on the case, 16 years and counting.

Legend gave its costume department a very important task: how do you tell Tom Hardy apart from Tom Hardy? Lead designer Caroline Harris and Soho tailor Chris Kerr solved it with buttons: Reggie, the charmer, wears single-breasted suits that feel safe and easy to trust. Ronnie, meanwhile, is the volatile one who gets three-piece double-breasted silhouettes with extra padding to make him look macho. On both sides, though, everything’s cut from vintage fabrics for a lived-in look straight from the hanger. Sixty years on, you could walk into any hotel bar dressed as either and nobody would bat an eye. Until you start talking business, that is.

Inspired by the real-world Cold War scandals that rocked British Intelligence, Tinker Tailor Soldier Spy follows the hunt for a Russian mole inside MI6 in 1973. So, as you might expect, it’s lots of men doing stuff in gray suits. Not Hardy’s Ricki Tarr, though. He’s the only agent with any personality, wardrobe-wise, all thanks to costume designer Jacqueline Durran. Her thinking? Steve McQueen on a mission: a shearling coat in Istanbul, a Baracuta Harrington in Paris, and not a standard suit in sight. The kind of menswear every guy on your FYP is wearing today—Hardy was just 15 years early.

In MobLand, Pierce Brosnan and Helen Mirren head up the Harrigans, an Irish crime family at war on several fronts—and no one knows it better than Hardy. He plays Harry Da Souza, the guy they call when things go wrong, which seems to happen fairly often. Costume designer Rebecca Hale gave him a wardrobe for the job: a battered wax jacket when he’s taking care of the Cotswolds estate, say, or a big overcoat when he’s doing business in the city. There’s one exception to the low-key menswear staples, though: his watch. In season one, he wore a solid gold Rolex GMT-Master II, which is reportedly the most valuable watch in the show. Pretty good for the hired help.

MobLand isn’t Hardy’s first Guy Ritchie gangster part. In RocknRolla, he’s Handsome Bob, the Wild Bunch’s getaway driver. And while everyone else looks like they’d had someone ‘”taken care of” before lunch, Hardy’s low-key menswear staples make him look more like the boy next door. A faded denim shirt. A classic bomber with jeans. A blue checked Western shirt, open just far enough. All simple pieces that let his charm do its thing, which it does when he sweet-talks a lawyer into revealing who’s been grassing to the police. Four years earlier, he played Clarkie in Layer Cake, another member of a London crime crew but one with a very different wardrobe: slicked hair, blazers, open shirts, brandy, and cigars. Similar JD, just with a very different wardrobe.

#6580. James Talarico’s Political-Powerhouse Girlfriend, Brianna Menard, Is Also a Master Thrifter

The 2026 midterm elections brought a blue wave of record-breaking wins. Among the most monumental was James Talarico, who became the first Democrat to win a Senate seat in Texas since 1988.

But his historic win is not all the internet has been talking about. At his celebratory Election Night event in Austin, Texas, the 37-year-old senator-elect was joined by his girlfriend, Brianna Menard, who was previously his chief of staff. Days later, the two attended a University of Texas football game—to which Menard wore a corduroy minidress she thrifted the week before—and the moment went viral across social media. (Even better, Talarico’s mom was the one who shared the photo of the couple at the game that quickly got traction on X.)

Chalk it up to her political prowess, clear sense of style, or the fact that she looks like an Instagram baddie, but some fans have even gone as far as to declare the pair this generation’s John F. Kennedy and Jacqueline Kennedy.

The couple first met in 2022, when Menard joined the politician’s team as chief of staff, and their relationship eventually turned romantic. Menard has been by Talarico’s side throughout his Senate campaign, accompanying him to the polls on Election Day in November and later joining him onstage to celebrate his historic victory.

So, who is Brianna Menard? Ahead, everything to know about Talarico’s accomplished and stylish girlfriend.

Menard is a born-and-raised Texan. She attended and graduated from the University of Texas at Austin, where she studied government.

In 2022, Menard was hired as Talarico’s chief of staff. According to reports, the two didn’t begin dating until a year later, after which Menard left her role working for the politician. In August 2026, Talarico’s campaign confirmed the couple’s relationship. “Four years ago, after he and his then-chief of staff developed feelings for each other and started dating, she left the office to pursue other opportunities,” a Talarico campaign spokesman told the New York Times.

After leaving Talarico’s office, Menard worked on the campaign for Rep. Salman Bhojani, the first Muslim member of the Texas legislature, and eventually became his chief of staff.

Menard is currently the director of TEXPAC and political education at the Texas Medical Association. According to her bio on the organization’s site, before stepping into the role, she had a decade of experience working in the Texas Legislature and previously served as executive director of the Early Childhood Caucus of the Texas House, where she led bipartisan efforts to “advance early learning, childcare access, workforce development, and healthcare affordability.”

Menard’s bio also lists her as the Board Secretary for Wheatsville Food Co-op and a member of the City of Austin’s Early Childhood Council.

According to a now-deleted bio on the Wheatsville Food Co-Op site, where she currently serves as Board Secretary, Menard called herself a “foodie and a committed vegan” who loves cooking. In the welcome post, she also revealed her love of yoga, dancing, and cuddling her cat named Zia.

“I’ve spent my career serving in the Texas Legislature and working on political campaigns across the state with the goal of making Texas a healthier and more equitable home for all of us,” she wrote in the post.

Menard joined Talarico at the polls to vote early in the midterm elections. On November 3, she also joined Talarico’s Election Night event in Austin, where she congratulated the Senator-elect on stage following his historic win. The couple shared a sweet hug and kiss to celebrate.

#6578. Move Over, Peeta! Here’s How ‘The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping’ Proves Haymitch is the Franchise’s Best Love Interest

The film gave us another glimpse at the former victor and it was game changing.

Back in the days of going to the bookstore at midnight to get a new book, young fans flocked to their nearest bookstores waiting to see if Katniss Everdeen chose Peeta Mellark or Gale Hawthorne in the hit YA trilogy The Hunger Games. Remember the Borders parties and how many of us would rock a Katniss braid? Those were the days! But the franchise has always been more than a typical young adult series.

From its conception, author Suzanne Collins taught young readers how to have hope in the face of destruction. President Coriolanus Snow wanted the world to fall at his feet and Katniss Everdeen taught audiences to fight back. Still, in the midst of her quest to free the citizens of Panem, a love story was present and it is something that made Suzanne's novels (and the subsequent movies) special.

The main love triangle of the original run of books and films centered around Katniss Everdeen and her love for both Gale Hawthorne and Peeta Mellark. While the question of who Katniss will choose loomed over the story as our beloved heroes fought for their lives, it felt like the one topic that fans talked about. “Are you Team Peeta or Team Gale?” Unless you were me. I was too busy crying over the death of Finnick Odair and how tragic his love story was. But, still, many of the other fans were focused on why either guy was best for Katniss. That doesn’t mean that was it though. There were other ships that called to us too.

In the middle of all the reapings and rebellions, fans realized one pairing had a different kind of history: Haymitch Abernathy and Effie Trinket. The two never had an explicit romance. Instead, the subtext between Effie and Haymitch led many fans to hope that there was a world where they would find love together. But their first meeting, as seen in The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping, is not where their will-they-won’t-they love story started. Instead, the prequel taught us more about Haymitch prior to his time in the arena and made it clear that the winner of the second Quarter Quell is one of the franchise’s best love interests.

This time played by Joseph Zada, fans finally got a glimpse into the love story that broke him. Lenore Dove Baird (Whitney Peak) was a Covey girl, a group of nomadic musicians that fans were first introduced to in the prequel novel and film The Ballad of Songbirds and Snakes. Haymitch fell in love with her prior to the start of Sunrise on the Reaping, even throwing himself in the line of fire to protect her, leading to him being forced to participate in the second Quarter Quell.

Like many other fans, in the original run of The Hunger Games, I wanted to know about Haymitch’s game. Mainly because we knew he survived by defeating double the amount of tributes that Katniss and Peeta did. It broke something in him. Or so we thought.

During the first trilogy, Haymitch was a drunk, seemingly uncaring about the tributes he was meant to mentor. But I saw something deeper in him, a sadness and loneliness that no one was willing to talk to him about—not that Haymitch would open up anyway. For years, we thought it was the turmoil of the games themselves that sent him into a spiral. Through Sunrise on the Reaping, we got insight into how Haymitch went from a young District 12 boy who actually swore off drinking to the lonely man that Peeta and Katniss encounter during their games. In fact, it’s the same reason why Haymitch actually might be the best love interest in the entire franchise (and yes, that does include the lover boy Finnick Odair himself): His dedication to Lenore Dove.

There is a deep love that all of the characters in The Hunger Games have for their chosen ones. But Haymitch’s dedication to Lenore Dove is rooted in his own grief, loneliness, and fear of hurting someone else he loves.

President Snow’s continued hatred of District 12 didn’t just reignite with Katniss. Instead, the ripples of his lost romance with Lucy Gray Baird (originally seen in The Ballad of Songbirds and Snakes) were thrown back in his face when Haymitch tried to break the games he was a part of. It meant that Snow had to send Haymitch a message and he did so in the most devastating way. Snow has Haymitch’s family killed and uses Haymitch’s own gift to Lenore Dove against him. All Haymitch wanted was to protect his family; instead they burn down his house with his family trapped inside and he was forced to hold his one true love in his arms as she died from the poison she unknowingly ingested that was hidden in the candy she thought Haymitch bought her. It’s no wonder why Haymitch isolates himself. He does so so that no one can be hurt by what the Capitol was willing to do to those he loved.

After seeing Sunrise on the Reaping, fans might think that Haymitch is actually more closely related to Finnick Odair in how they both show their love for someone by protecting them in any way possible. Still, Haymitch’s anguish over Lenore Dove kept him alone and separated from the rest of District 12 out of love. Finnick and Mags went into the game during Catching Fire because they both were protecting Annie. Haymitch isolated and drank himself into a stupor for the same reason.

What can I say? I love two boys who are willing to hurt themselves to keep others safe. And aren’t going to just paint their faces to blend into foliage. (I’m so sorry, Peeta. I do love you, I promise!)

In many ways, Haymitch is the very definition of a “lover boy.” One who loved so deeply that the loss of it changed who he was. Still, the beauty of it shows why Haymitch was so cautious about caring for other people. He wasn’t cold or broken, he was afraid of what President Snow would do to those around him.

Seeing how Haymitch was before the Second Quarter Quell and what he was willing to do for his love of Lenore Dove? It set the standard for what a love interest was willing to do much higher than before. It also means that Peeta Mellark needs to step up his game if he wants to get on Haymitch’s level. “If not for the baby,” isn’t going to cut it when Haymitch is ready to take down an entire empire for love.

#6576. Rihanna and A$AP Rocky Make a Stylish Outing in Big Coats and Big Pants

Every product on this page was chosen by a Harper's BAZAAR editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

When it comes to stylish couples, Rihanna and A$AP Rocky are a match made in fashion heaven. Ever since their relationship turned romantic over five years ago, the pair have made it seem like they’ve been pulling from a his-and-hers closet. From their dramatic plush coats at the Met Gala to their matching olive moment on the Black Panther: Wakanda Forever red carpet, their coordinating looks are always on point. But, even more impressively, they always manage to complement one other without losing their individual sense of style.

On Monday afternoon, that impressive display continued as the stars were seen in Beverly Hills, California, where the dress code of the day appeared to be “open jackets and big pants.” It was the first time in weeks that the pair has been seen in public together, as A$AP has been on his Don’t Be Dumb Tour across Europe for the past couple of months.

For the outing, Rihanna curated a watermelon color scheme up top, layering a soft pink T-shirt underneath an army-green parka, presumably from Celine.

The utility-style outerwear featured rows of pockets in front and a plush brown shearling collar that also lined the inside of the jacket and cuffs. Notably, Rihanna may have borrowed the coat from her stylist, Jahleel Weaver, as he posted an Instagram Story yesterday afternoon in which he was seen wearing the exact same one.

To complete her fit, the “Diamonds” singer wore baggy white jeans and a pair of strappy white stilettos. Oh, and our queen of statement glasses added a set of cranberry-colored visor shades.

For his part, Rocky stacked a dark-wash jean jacket and a creamy corduroy one over a black tee, completing the fit with obsidian wide-leg slacks and inky leather Oxfords. As for his sunglasses, the Ray-Ban creative director chose a pair of the brand’s classic aviators.

The couple’s outing comes days after their daughter, Rocki, made her high-fashion debut in a new Dior campaign, proving that every member of this family really is programmed to have incredible style.

#6569. Lea Michele Wants a Scream Queens Reboot, Too

The star of Kiehl’s new “Gift Like a Local” campaign also reflects on the legacy of Glee.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

With multiple projects on the horizon, all eyes are on Lea Michele. Glee is getting a reboot; fans are begging for Scream Queens to come back, and the actress and singer is about to embark on a tour with Chess co-star Aaron Tveit. She also just starred in Kiehl’s new “Gift Like a Local” campaign. In partnership with Uber, Michele traveled around to small businesses in New York City and doled out Kiehl’s products. The Glee star even picked most of the stops herself. “Kiehl’s had emailed me and they said, ‘We really want to feature some local businesses in this campaign. Can you send us some of your thoughts?’” she says. “It took me about 30 seconds. I had an email with 50 places.”

It’s a natural collaboration, as the Bronx-born Michele is the perfect shepherd for a curated NYC experience. In many ways, she breathes New York. She’s starred on Broadway multiple times, most recently in Chess, which just received a Grammy nomination for Best Musical Theater Album. In the later seasons of Glee, her character, Rachel Berry, moved to New York to chase her Broadway dreams (Michele is also rumored to be returning to the show’s reboot). She currently lives in New York with her husband and two children. On the morning of our interview, fresh off a sold-out show with Tveit at the Beacon Theater, she’s having a classic New York morning. “I took my kids to school. I was walking in the sunshine, I had my matcha. I’m getting ready for an event right now,” she says. “It just is a great day.”

Below, Michele talks about Glee, her collaboration with Kiehl’s, and whether she’d ever come back for a Scream Queens reboot.

Aaron [Tveit] would love my personal favorite, the Grapefruit Body Cleanser. That’s just my tried-and-true. I used it this morning. If you’re feeling a little tired, it helps you to wake up and feel good. But even if you’re not, if you’re feeling like how I felt this morning, energized and happy, it’s just one of my favorite ways to start the day. Love, love, love their body lotion. It’s one of the only body lotions that I feel like actually works for me and helps to really moisturize my skin. They have all these amazing little kits as well over the holidays. I’ve given my dad Kiehl’s lip balm and eye cream in his stockings every year for the holidays.

I’m really excited to be a part of the Kiehl’s family now. I love our commercial that we shot and the fact that we had the opportunity to support some of the local small businesses.

I grew up here. I was born in the Bronx. My father owned a deli here in the city. We had ice cream parlors and candy shops. We featured Élan Flowers in my neighborhood. Seasons in Times Square is somewhere that I go all the time and buy flowers for people in Broadway shows. I love Onda, it’s an amazing little facial and wellness store in Tribeca. Obviously, so many little coffee shops. I mean, the list is quite long.

I just love everything white. I love all-white. My house is all white, but peonies are my favorite flowers. I love hydrangeas. I love white roses. I just love a big white bouquet of flowers.

[Aaron Tveit] and I just found out this morning that almost the entire tour sold out. [It] took less than 24 hours. At our show last night, I think we learned so much about what the show is and what the show could be. We had to cut 10 songs from the concert because we just have so much material. I don’t think that we’re going to do the same show every night. I think that each city is going to get their own show. We’re going to do some of what I would say are our greatest hits together, and some of our individual hits that I think that I will always do in concert, but I really just feel like there’s so much that we can do.

I want Radio City to be epic. We haven’t really talked about having some friends join us, but that’s also something that we could definitely do if we wanted to and bring out some friends to sing some songs with us. The options are endless.

[Aaron is] one of my dearest friends. His wife is one of my closest friends. He’s friends with my husband. Our children are friends. [It’s] amazing that I get to work with someone that I just respect and care about so much.

It feels awesome. Normally I know when these things are happening, but my publicist texted me in the morning. She was like, “You got a Grammy nomination.” I cried. My reaction was so, even for me, surprising and unexpected. I just broke down. My husband was walking our kids out the door to take them to school, and I was like, “I can’t believe this. We got nominated for a Grammy.” It means so much. I can’t tell you the talent of the cast of Chess. Every single cast member, the most extraordinary talent, our ensemble, the band. To have the cast get this incredible honor, it’s beyond well deserved. I think every single person who saw Chess would agree.

We are constantly one-upping each other in the wellness world. He’ll be like, “Have you seen this? Do you have this?” He had some sort of, I don’t know if it was an infrared mat, and I was like, “Okay, I need that. What’s that?” He saw that I had an amazing water filter in my house, and he’s like, “Okay, what’s that? I need that.” He is probably one of the only people who could rival me in my biohacking, wellness, and taking care of myself.

Most of the time during Chess, if we weren’t talking about Summer House and all of the drama, we were talking about what are all of the wellness things that we’re doing and to try to stay healthy.

I’ve been doing Pilates for years. Emma Roberts took me to my first Pilates class when we were living in New Orleans together, shooting Scream Queens. We went to Romney Studios and did a private lesson. I fell in love with it. Since then, I’ve done mat Pilates and hot Pilates and power Pilates. Everything. I felt pretty good going into this, being like, “I know what I need to know.”

It wasn’t my first time playing a killer on television, which was also kind of funny. It was so awesome to return to television last night after so many years, and I just had a really great time.

I met a little girl last night at the meet-and-greet. She was probably 7 years old, and she came to see the concert with her two dads. Her dads were like, “We just are starting to show her some of the musical numbers.” I was like, “Look at you, you’re a little Rachel Berry.” She had her headband on and her cute little penny loafers, and it was just the sweetest thing. What we all created, and what Ryan and Brad and Ian created with Glee, was just so amazing. The things that people say to me about how much it affected their life and brought them so much joy means the world to me.

I hear stories like that from people all the time, and I know that it really means so much to all of us.

The love for Scream Queens has always been there. Emma [Roberts] and I love that show. We talk about it all the time. Ryan knows, too. Ryan and I talk about it always and I beg him all the time. I’m like, “Can we please bring Scream Queens back?” Ryan should do it. It would be awesome.

It’s so beautiful. I talked a lot about Spring Awakening during my concert last night. We are celebrating 20 years of Spring Awakening opening on Broadway this December. We have a group chat with the original cast. Myself, Lauren Pritchard, and Jonathan Groff are planning a really special private night just for the original cast, a little celebration. I’m so excited that it’s coming to Off-Broadway. My dear, dear, dear sister, Lilli Cooper, is going to be in it. Spring Awakening is such a beautiful and important show, and so anytime that it has the opportunity for people to see it, it should be there. It’s the most beautiful piece of art.

Oh, yeah. I’ve had the opportunity and the real honor to be a part of some incredible original Broadway productions, both with Ragtime and Spring Awakening. It’s an incredible, incredible show.

There are so many. I want to do everything, I really do. I want to do it all. If I am lucky enough to perform on Broadway for the rest of my life, then that would just be the greatest dream. There are so many great shows that have been on Broadway that could be revived and amazing pieces and incredibly talented artists that are writing original pieces as well. So, I’m ready and available for whatever is to come.

This interview has been edited and condensed for clarity.

#6567. The 40 Best Thanksgiving Episodes to Watch When You're Too Full to Move

This year, I am mostly just thankful for television.

Thanksgiving is around the corner, which means it's time for too much turkey, naps facedown on the floor while everyone begs you to help with the dishes, at least one relative saying something problematic, and texting your hometown ex to "see how they are." In other words, it's the coziest time of year! And if you're looking to amp up said cozy vibes by a factor of ten, we highly recommend crawling into bed (and/or some sort of wearable blanket situation) and firing up anywhere from 1-10 (or 40) iconic Thanksgiving episodes of television.

There's truly something for everyone on this list—whether you want to watch every single Friends Thanksgiving episode, enjoy the Thanksgiving misadventures of Bob Belcher, or kick back with a classic Thanksgiving episode of Gossip Girl. Because yes, there are multiple.

Oh, and if you like your Thanksgiving episodes dripping with wealth, there's also The Sopranos and Succession. No matter which ep(s) you press play on, we highly recommend enjoying them with a vodka cranberry in hand (counts as cranberry sauce, fight me), as well as a full pumpkin pie. What happens between you and said pumpkin pie is your business! Happy Thanksgiving to one and all!

If your family makes a point of watching the Macy's Thanksgiving Day Parade every year, you'll appreciate the suitably wacky plotline in this classic Seinfeld episode. In the days leading up to Turkey Day, Kramer gives away all of Jerry's shoes, George attempts to purchase a car once owned by Jon Voight, and Elaine gets her boss a ticket to pilot the Woody the Woodpecker balloon at NYC's famous parade.

No Thanksgiving is complete without an episode of Bob's Burgers, and this one takes the cake for its hilarious depiction of meal prep gone wrong (read: Bob's turkey finds its way—mysteriously—to the toilet).

In this hilarious episode of Fresh Off the Boat, Jessica finally gets the chance to prove to her mother that she can handle the Thanksgiving meal. Of course, nothing's ever that easy in a sitcom, and getting something on the table proves nearly impossible.

Mindy attends Thanksgiving dinner at her friend Gwen's, where she encounters her worst nightmare in the form of her former blind date, Dennis, and his new Indian girlfriend. Thankfully, Mindy always knows what to say—and she never holds back.

It wouldn't be a rerun binge without a dose of Cheers, right? In this Thanksgiving classic, most of the crew have no place to go for the holiday, so they throw a potluck at Carla's. But an uncooked turkey, a frustrated Frasier, and last min changes to the guest list throw the day into disarray.

Thanksgiving is practically synonymous with football, and Friday Night Lights, naturally, leans all the way in. The gang gets together for a feast ahead of the big Friday night game, and it's up to Landry to make the game-winning field goal.

PLL is not necessarily the cheery holiday fare you're used to on Thanksgiving Day, but if you need a captivating tale to distract you while the pies bake, this episode is perfect. There's holiday decorating, pumpkin pie-making...and, oh, murder!

So this isn't technically a Thanksgiving episode, but the Harvest Festival is the closest the Parks and Rec crew gets to celebrating Turkey Day onscreen. Plus, a "curse" is involved.

After learning about class divisions in Rwanda at school, Cory and Shawn decide to be progressive and bridge the gap between their own socioeconomic statuses by...uh, having Thanksgiving at the Hunters' trailer park? Sure!

When Will's mom, Viola, arrives in Bel-Air for Thanksgiving, she causes a fuss when she criticizes Vivian for letting the kids be lazy during the holiday. Hurt, Vivian makes the kids cook Thanksgiving dinner, which—surprise, surprise—turns into a hilarious, but heartwarming, disaster.

Danger's always lurking around the corner in Riverdale, even on Thanksgiving Day. An ice storm forces the community into lockdown, and when Archie decides to throw a feast for underprivileged kids, some unexpected guests arrive.

Blair Waldorf is the HBIC of Thanksgiving, and that's a fact. Although every single season of Gossip Girl serves us a delicious Thanksgiving episode, season 1 sets the bar high with flashbacks of Serena drunk (in broad daylight) the year before, a look into Blair's past, and the reveal of Rufus and Lily's v. complicated history.

This Thanksgiving episode of Grey's Anatomy might be the most relatable television to ever exist. Izzie wants to have the perfect Turkey Day, but she is literally clueless and has no idea what to do. I mean, you can’t really blame her. This episode aired in 2005 and Pinterest wasn't even invented until 2010. How do you cook anything without Pinterest?

Dwight deals with some childhood trauma the only way he knows how...by creating an elaborate hay festival so he can finally be crowned Hay King. Also, Ryan tries to scam everyone out of a ton of money. Festive!

There are 10 Thanksgiving episodes of Friends, but this one is the best, thanks to Monica, Joey, Chandler, Phoebe, Rachel, and Ross sitting around sharing their holiday horror stories. Comedic gold.

Ahh, nothing makes you realize your crazy family isn't so awful like spending Thanksgiving in solitary confinement or eating dinner with a spork. Right, Piper and Alex?

Rory and Lorelai attend FOUR Turkey Day feasts, which actually sounds like a total nightmare given the fact that they had to wear real clothes and couldn't take post-meal naps on the couch.

Everyone's trying to either skip out on dinner, avoid dressing up, or troll each other's ability to cook. Basically, it's the Modern Family crew living out your absolute best Thanksgiving dreams.

There's really only one reason to watch this, and it's Uncle Jesse (in all his first-season, borderline-mullet glory) cooking a turkey in a leather vest. Enjoy!

In which Jacqueline says she's thankful for "loving someone other than herself…and revenge," which honestly, we should all aspire to.

#6566. How Am I Supposed to Survive the Apocalypse Without My Phone?

Anyone who’s ever been trapped on a stalled subway or a WiFi-less flight knows how screwed we might feel without access to our phones. Last week’s tech outages––first, a failure of Microsoft’s systems, then, a national grounding of flights, followed by problems from Charles Schwab to Apple iCloud––were a reminder of how fragile our digital infrastructure is, especially at the dawn of AI. Preppers on Reddit, better known for discussing generators and the storage of food, began talking about how to AI–apocalypse proof the devices we’re all so dependent on. “getting serious about my preps with all the cyber attacks going on,” one user wrote. “I work for a security company so I get a front row seat to a lot of the chaos. I’m specifically prepping for prolonged loss of power.”

Many found their way to Nomad, a pocket-sized device created to aid in offline situations. “I had huge, huge spikes in traffic,” said Jackson Studner, creator of Nomad and a student at Millersville University of Pennsylvania. (“I’m not a company or a startup, just a college student who enjoys building useful things and sharing them,” Studner’s website reads.) With the help of his professors, he built Nomad to provide some entertainment on camping trips and cross-country trips with friends. They’d hit a dead zone, and realize they wanted to watch a movie, or run video game software. Nomad is a miniscule server with TV and films, the entire Wikipedia library, and portable Wi-Fi, and offline maps. “I really like the reliability aspect,” Studner tells me. “I like to own my stuff. It's kind of that simple.” He’s also added navigation for his new community preparing for the apocalypse (or at least a grid shutdown). “It will actually tell you how to go along the road, it can tell you how to walk there, it'll calculate times, that kind of stuff,” he said.

Elsewhere, there are emergency libraries that you can download to your existing computer, with offline chatbots that can tell you how to find clean water or start a fire: lastbook.org, which includes how-to videos; Survival LLM, which offers up information from the CDC and FEMA; and Project Nomad (different from Studner’s Nomad), from Chris Sherwood, a prominent YouTuber. Sherwood is best known for posting explainer videos showing viewers how to build their “homelabs”—personal, at-home computing devices—under the name Crosstalk Solutions.

The interest in Project Nomad, Sherwood said, has been overwhelming. “It’s got over 40,000 stars on GitHub, which means that 40,000 people not only saw the project and were on the page, but they clicked the little button to favorite it. And I'm getting over 200,000 views on the website per month,” he said.

Despite his new fans, Sherwood himself isn’t a prepper. He was inspired by the Information Technology Disaster Resource Center (ITDRC), a non-profit that goes into disasters and provides internet services when they are completely down. “I had the opportunity to go on deployment with [ITDRC] when Hurricane Ian came through, and I saw firsthand all these people that were offline. It was a disaster. Getting people back online was almost as important as the food and water supplies that were getting set up to help with the recovery,” he explained.

Studner wasn’t a prepper either–– at first. But since Nomad took off and he’s found himself in more “prepper” corners of the internet, his feelings have slightly changed. “It’s for peace of mind. I have my little go bag. I'm not too, too worried about it, but I like to know that if there ever was a real problem, I'd be okay in that situation.”

#6565. The Princess of Wales Swaps Her Classic Waves for a Romantic Boho Hairstyle: How to Recreate It

During her three-day tour of India, the Princess of Wales attended the 2026 Earthshot Prize Ceremony in Mumbai. Prince William founded the initiative in 2020, and it awards environmental solutions with grants to scale and advance their projects.

For the event, Kate wore an oxblood, floor-length Anarkali dress by designer Mahima Mahajan and paired it with extra-fluffy, voluminous bohemian curls pinned in a half-updo.

An elegant, extra-glossy wavy hairstyle has long been a beauty signature for the princess, often teamed with a side part, hats, or a polished ponytail. But for the green carpet in India, the royal opted for a much more dramatic style that matches the glamorous mood of her outfit, with tighter, cascading bohemian curls woven into a beautiful half-updo twist for a romantic finish.

"I would create these curls using a medium-sized tong," recommends Philipp Haug, co-founder of Haug London Haus salon. "Take relatively small sections and use the tongs with a twisting technique, curling one section away from the face and alternating with one section towards the face throughout. Slightly root stretch when you come to the top of the head instead of curling too close to the scalp."

To enhance the fluffiness and volume of the curls, shake them out with your fingers instead of combing them.

#6564. 10 Sheer, Minimalist Winter Nail Ideas, From Lavender Cream to Soft Stardust

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

For subtle winter manicure inspiration, look no further.

The allure of soft, understated nails is going nowhere. Rather, throughout 2026, we’ve seen the minimalist manicure movement gain momentum with each new season. “It’s the biggest shift I’ve seen in my chair this year,” says celebrity nail technician Iram Shelton. She explains that the theme has been “natural nails, but better,” which is centered around low-maintenance colors and finishes.

For winter, this translates to sheer, milky tones, short, considered nail shapes and microvariations of wearable nail art. In preparation for party season, expect to see minuscule flecks of metallic gold and star-themed motifs, too. While milky pink nails will always be in style, this winter, it’s all about unexpected sheers like lavender, bronze, and pearly white. Not only do these colors go with everything, but they also are extremely forgiving—you can paint them on yourself without a problem.

Londontown Illuminating Nail Concealer in Bare

Essie Gel Couture Long-Lasting Nail Polish in Liquid Diamonds

Below, find 10 of the most polished, pared-back nail ideas to bookmark for your next appointment.

Last season, we had Hailey Bieber milk nails, followed by Sienna Miller’s ‘Chantilly cream’. Now? Lavender takes the spotlight, in its dreamiest, near-translucent form. A high-shine top coat makes this tone appear even hazier in the light.

Leaning into minimalism doesn’t necessarily mean forgoing color. Take this rich, berry red for proof; when an all-over pigment isn’t on the cards, a sleek micro French tip is equally flattering.

For a simpler take on autumn’s ‘gold fairy dust’ nail trend (as seen on Taylor Swift), draw inspiration from stardust instead. Here, a natural blushed pink base sits beautifully under a gently dazzling, golden shimmer top coat.

Traditional French tips are given a modern, metallic upgrade without veering into futuristic territory. When reserved for the base of the nail, a gold-toned cuff is eye-catching but in a subtle way.

Frosted pearlescent finishes are as luminous as they are delicate—not to mention destined for winter weather. When worn with a cozy cashmere knit, you’d be hard-pressed to find a more perfect pairing.

Square and squoval nails are seeing a revival this season. Keep your shapes cropped, softly rounded, and ultra-glossy if committing to minimalism.

Trade in winter's deep, vibrant mocha, espresso, and cocoa-inspired browns for a sheer bronze pigment. When kept close to the natural nail color, this shade is the best way to embrace elevated color without going overboard.

While the notion of nail art might suggest something elaborate colorful, petite star motifs make a case for the opposite. One on each nail balances a feeling of festivity with practicality for everyday wear.

Granted, a white Christmas might be far on the horizon. But this set sees micro polka dots get a dainty, wintry upgrade, inspired by tiny droplets of snow.

If a micro French isn’t your style, an ombré is worth considering. Begin with a nude beige-pink base color, and watch it blend seamlessly into a cozy, seasonal color.

#6562. Best Men's Studded Belts 2026: From RRL to Our Legacy

Most people might build an outfit around a pair of shoes. Others, around a jacket. But few, surprisingly, do it around a belt.

At GQ, we’ve always said that the overlooked belt is more than the sum of its buckle and holes. Not only will it stop your slacks from going south, but a good leather belt can make just as much of a statement as your shoes, and deliver as much of a stylistic flourish as a tie. Unconvinced? Enter: The studded belt. (Which, coincidentally, we're now increasingly seeing be offered by some of the most imitated brands in the industry, from Lemaire to RRL, Mfpen to Our Legacy.)

The charm of the studded belt is that it’s a vibe chameleon. Its decorative elements can pay tribute to any number of aesthetics, from Americana to punk to European artistry. And while any one will easily provide further flair to an already-singing outfit, the best ones also single-handedly bring even the most minimalist wardrobe to life. (And to be honest, that’s how I feel studded belts are best worn today: Against a white tee and jeans, for an easy win without changing your whole look.)

To help you find the right one for you, I've sorted through the vast waist-wear market to find a selection that won't just make your outfits sing, but set them on course for superstardom. See you out there, Stud.

Let's start gently: Somehow, Anderson’s has made a studded belt that whispers. It’s a small but mighty addition to their lovingly handcrafted Italian leather goods.

“If a stud falls out, make a wish,” is the outlook over at Htc Los Angeles—and they provide plenty of it. A true purveyor of the style, the brand has been specializing in rock-and-roll embellished leather goods since the ’90s. And they aren’t stingy with it either.

Trust Corridor NYC to come in clutch for all of your folksy Americana needs. The worn-in look of their studded belt—made in full-grain leather and tarnished hardware—carries all the tales of someone who’s worn it through the arid badlands.

If the fits over at the Ralph Lauren PoloFest in Tokyo last month have inspired you to join the RL tribe, any one of the many studded belts from RRL (Ralph's ranch-minded line) is a strong place to start.

As you’ll see from our list, Our Legacy—a staple for any serious fashion guy—has secured two call-outs. Here, they cut diamond studs to form a snakeskin-like pattern that, when worn against your best corduroy pants, gives the look some severe shine.

While we’re talking about studded belts as an easy hardware win, they're also the perfect place to score some color points too. Here, the green and orange mixes it up more than traditional turquoise.

Since its founding in 2012, Maximum Henry has been handmaking our favorite leather goods right here in New York City, including their beloved oval buckle belt. But they also know how to do a good, uncomplicated studded belt.

The floral studs on this OL belt aren’t merely decorative, but are punched into the very function of the eyelets. It’s a sweet look, really, and one done so effortlessly.

Dressing for winter, when all you ever want to feel is cozy, can leave you feeling like a schlub. There is, however, an easy fix in the form of the enviably Parisian stylings of Lemaire, who’ve released a brown belt with almond-shaped studs. Wear it with your all-denim outfit or heavy grey wool pants, it’ll make you feel like one of the brand’s cityslicking models in their closely-studied runway shows.

While we’ve so far been paying attention to how studs can add some play, texture still plays a key role to make an outfit sing. A Kind of Guise made their turquoise studded belt with a tan nubuck leather.

Lemaire might be better known for their artsy fabrics and drape-y cuts, but their accessories department is a treasure trove too.

Florals can be a lot generally for most guys, but Mfpen uses theirs sparingly on their lean 2cm wide belt. Made in patent brown leather, it also adds a nice formal sheen to a dark winter ensemble.

We’ve so far seen how studded belts can sway from Americana to minimalist vibes, but we can’t leave out the heavy metal rockers. Heliot Emil shaves off the edges for something more digestible, but still packs enough punch to cut through a monochromatic outfit.

Finally, we've talked a lot about how to add spice to your fit, but what does that mean—really? Eraldo has some answers.
